* **A fan-out lane returns focused evidence; the integrator owns repair and the complete gate for a stable integration batch.** Follow [the tool guide's check schedule](../../tools/README.md#check-scheduling-during-fan-out): batch document edits before a checker run, target changed rules and tool behavior, and report drift and deferred checks without each lane launching bare `run.py`, full selftests or `--fix`. The integrator settles the batch's authored inputs, repairs the derived figures and runs one complete required wave over the integrated tree. A known-red checker baseline postpones selftesting; it supplies no mutation figure. Handoff remains provisional until the batch meets every landing condition, including the full mutation verdict where the tier requires it; lane evidence names its tested inputs and is never presented as the integrated verdict.
* **A count in a note carries the predicate it was taken with, and a figure the revision it was taken at.** Two readers counting one document at one revision can both be right and disagree, a case-sensitive pattern and a strict bullet head giving different totals, so a count is reproducible only with its predicate beside it; and a figure read off a working tree that a peer's push can move between two commands is reproducible only with its hash. F-208 is the class booked once, a search's result stated as a smaller set than the search returns.
* **The width test is set and it is a span floor: a class-X range's upper end is at least twice its lower end.** That is the *roughly a factor of two* this document already claims of every range, made a gate for the one class whose outturn says it is not decoration, and `tools/check.py` holds it. Class I carries no floor, having run under estimate.
  **The obvious threshold was tried first and is arithmetically unavailable, which is why this one is a span rather than a multiple of the midpoint.** *The calibrated value lies inside the range*, upper ≥ 1.68 × midpoint, is the test the ratio actually motivates; but a midpoint is the mean of the range ends, so that test is equivalent to lower ≤ 0.32 × midpoint and forces every class-X range to a span above five. Applied to the cells as they stand it would either drive the lower ends to a third of their midpoints or, holding the lower ends, re-price the midpoints upward by more than a factor of two, which is exactly the re-price the ratio is stated **not** to be. The two rules collide, and the span floor is what survives the collision: it is tied to the convention this document already states, it discriminates the class the measurement discriminates, and it leaves the calibrated total where it belongs, stated separately rather than folded in.
  **What the floor does not do is make the calibration a gate.** A cell can clear the span and still have a midpoint the ratio says is low, and nothing here reports that; the calibrated total is where it shows, and the 1.42 the whole convention rests on is fitted over three completed items, a count the record carries and no span widens.
* **`Wasm-parallel` is a legacy label on completed work, not a required execution route.** Gallina authoring needs its reviewed contract and proof environment, not a target compiler or image. Use `run.py proofs` and the applicable generated campaigns; use the Wasm oracle where the item's comparison explicitly requires it. M1.5 supplies the demonstration loop and M4.3 the staged IPC client. Another staging effort belongs to its consumer and is never silently required of every proof artifact.

* **Landing is two-tier.** Tier A is a full attended read: any register, profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix edit; any moved co-read pair; any new normative claim; every decision item. Tier B is a spot read of the item's findings, admitted on four mechanical conditions: checker green with every mutant killed, no Tier-A edit, the co-read ledger unmoved, and a rule holding whatever fact the item created.
* **A landing declares its tier, and a Tier-B landing names what holds it.** A landed item carries one `Landed: Tier A` or `Landed: Tier B under` line in its completion-log note, the second naming in bold every rule that holds a fact the landing created. **A bolded rule id is a holder citation and a plain one is not**: a rule relied on is written in bold beside what it holds, which is the form the landed items that created one already use, and a rule merely discussed stays plain, so the document tells the two apart instead of leaving it to the reader. A holder need not be new, an existing rule whose subject a landing extends holding the new member and being named the same way. K-84 holds every holder citation to naming a rule [the registry](../../tools/check-rules.md) carries and every Tier-B declaration to naming one, and it reads a declaration where it is made rather than demanding one where it is absent, a tier being a property of the reading a landing was given and not something a later hand assigns to it. What no rule decides is whether the named rule holds *that* fact, which is the residue every conferral here declares.
* **An item's acceptance predicate is authored and landed before its implementation lane opens.** M0.11 and M1.8a are the precedent and M1.8a states the reason: an instrument arriving with its own acceptance criteria has graded its own homework. In a multi-lane operation this is what lets a lane self-check, and it is the precondition for a Tier-B landing. **The five open items that owed one carry it**, authored at S18 rather than left for a lane to discover: M1.7, M4.4 and M5.3, which are on the critical chain and where the precondition is worth the most, and M6.3b and M6.5b behind the M8a gate. Where a predicate is not yet writable the cell says so and names what owes it, as M3.6b does and as three of those five now do over one of their halves, rather than staying silent. An item entered after this convention carries its predicate at entry, and one whose predicate is refused carries the refusal in the same cell.
* **Validation is generated, not authored, wherever an oracle exists.** The model-as-oracle technique is proven in this tree at 21,546 vectors (M2.1) and 658,659 vectors with twelve seeded defects killed (R1a). A hand-written corpus member where a generator would serve is a finding.
* **A fact has one owner and every other statement of it is generated.** Where an artifact restates a fact the model, the register or a contract owns, it is emitted from that owner and regenerated in the checking act. A hand-written restatement held equal by a rule is the older discipline and is a finding under this one: the rule can only tell you the two disagree, after they already do.
* **Separate start dependencies from integration dependencies.** Each open item's dispatch note names **Start** (the exact contract or artifact needed to author it), **Owns** (its deliverable and write boundary), **Check** (its acceptance predicate and focused command where available), and **Join** (the producer outputs needed for integration). Its **Execute** note states the reuse, work split or early rejection strategy; the shared procedure in [Sequencing](#sequencing) applies to every task. A missing contract or acceptance predicate blocks the affected implementation, not unrelated siblings. Contract review may open immediately; the implementation follows its landed predicate. A fixture or abstract interface permits local checking but never substitutes for the real producer at the join.
* **Split by independently checkable output.** A shared type, schema or calling convention gets one owner and lands before consumers change it; consumers then work in separate modules against that contract. Within an existing estimate, named work packages may be assigned separately, but the parent stays open until all its acceptance clauses hold. Independent completion credit requires child cells re-priced at entry, with no estimate on their parent; merely exposing concurrency changes neither the estimate nor the evidence.
* **`after the M8a gate` is a priority deferral unless the item names an artifact that only that gate supplies.** Independent contract, host-logic and test-harness work may use spare capacity before M8a once its own start conditions hold. This does not move its delivery into M8a, add it to the boot roster, authorize a scope-cut proof, or override scalar-first and measured-need admission of new datapaths. Final freeze decisions and measurements still wait for their complete corpus and required inputs.
* **Every fan-out subagent performs all repository work in its own dedicated Git worktree**, including scouts, reviewers, document workers and nested subagents. Before dispatch, the parent uses the repository's worktree command to create a fresh lane or verifies a dedicated host-provisioned checkout against the intended input revision; the brief names its absolute path, branch or detached HEAD, base, owned files, focused checks and integrator. Each call explicitly targets that worktree, and the integrator alone writes the integration checkout. The [tool guide's isolation procedure](../../tools/README.md#worktree-isolation-during-fan-out) owns placement and lifecycle for every platform and model.
* `Parallel` means the item can proceed subject to its dispatch note rather than to its position in this document; each assigned subagent uses its own worktree and any build outputs belong to that worktree's lane.
* **`Parallel` is about the build lane and not about attention.** A linked worktree derives its own lane under `/root/build/lane-<name>/`, one directory holding that lane's build tree, iterate tree and SMT cache ([I7](#build-loop-instruments)), so two items that both write `model/` share no build tree, no log, and no emulator, and a build holds its own lane rather than the toolchain: a second `run.py model build` over a live one in the same lane is refused by name, while a sibling lane's runs beside it. **Lanes isolate files and build trees, not CPU.** The machine stays one thing, twelve cores under one WSL VM, so concurrent model work is priced in wall time rather than in correctness. A provenance decision, document act or checker rule can use its own worktree without starting a model build; its checks still share the machine's capacity.
* **One integrator owns shared landing files, the review queue and gate scheduling.** Implementation lanes return their commit identity, inputs, focused evidence, findings, deferred checks and proposed completion note; the integrator reconciles the checklist, completion log, generated arithmetic and any shared registration files. The dispatch note's **Check** names the lane's focused commands and the integrator's remaining gates. Reserve the Tier-A read before starting work that needs it, and prioritize contracts that release consumers and repairs on the M8a path. Keep the integrated checkout stable through validation, share its verdict across the batch, and rerun checks when their inputs change or a failure needs investigation. Separate worktrees do not make overlapping edits, CPU capacity or shared mutable toolchain installs independent. `run.py seed properties` has exclusive use of the checkout it mutates, including against readers and gate runs; schedule other work in a different checkout with isolated outputs.
* Later milestones remain deliverable-level until entry.

### Current summary

These figures sum the priced reference-build, assessment and named hardening cells. They are not a total for the end-to-end proof program. The full compiler/security-preservation, native refinement/admission, binary constant-time, WCET, storage crash/concurrency/liveness composition and RTL-to-Sail proofs are not fully costed here. Physical fabrication, laboratory calendar and additional qualification campaigns also lie outside these engineering cells. Q22g owns pricing the unassigned proof/specification slices; the existing hardware items keep their measured-device and laboratory boundaries. Completed actuals and the separate execution clocks retain the conventions above.

* Completed: S1, S2, S3, S4, S7, S8, S9, S10a, S10b, S11, S12, S13a, S13b, S14, S15, S16, S17, S18, S19, S20, S21, S22, S23, S24, S25, S26, S27, S28, S29, S30, Q1, Q2a, Q2c, Q3a, Q4a, Q5a, Q11, Q14, Q16, Q17, Q18, Q20a, Q21, Q22a, Q22b, Q22c, Q22f, M0.1–M0.5, M0.6a, M0.6b, M0.6c (c1–c4), M0.6d, M0.6e (e1–e5), M0.6f, M0.6g, M0.6h, M0.7, M0.8a, M0.8b, M0.8d, M0.9, M0.10, M0.11, M0.12, M0.13, M0.14, M0.15, M0.16, M0.17, M0.18, M0.19, M0.20, M1.1, M1.1a, M1.1b, M1.2a, M1.4′, M1.5, M1.6, M1.8a, M1.9, M3.1, M3.2, M3.3, M3.4a, M3.4d, M3.6a, M4.1, M4.1a, M4.1b, M4.2a, M4.2b, M4.3, M5.1, M5.2, M6.0a, M6.0b, M6.1a, M6.2a, M6.3a, M6.4, M6.5a, R1a, R1c-i, R4, and the build-loop instruments I0, I2, I3, I4, I5, I6, I7, I8, I9, I10, I11, I12, I13, I14, I15, I16 and the initial check/emit/FAST tooling. Five instrument clauses are **declines carrying a measurement**, the act of measuring having removed the premise: I2, I6 and I8 whole, I4's second clause, and I5's prefix-map clause. **S14 is a sixth of the same kind and the first outside the instruments**, S9 having retired the first of the two grounds I8 declined push CI on and the measurement then finding the second standing.
* The open work and the order it is taken in are [Sequencing](#sequencing)'s; each landed item's note is in [the completion log](completion-log.md), which also keeps [the lane-by-lane state this summary carried](completion-log.md#the-summarys-state-before-s10b-landed-2026-09-05) before the notes moved out of this file.
* Total estimate: 1,592.5 h midpoint, class I 230 h and class X 885.5 h over the open items.
* Calibrated against completed-item outturn (class I 0.71, class X 1.42): approximately 1,897.7 h.
* Progress by estimate: 477 of 1,592.5 h complete (30.0%); 1,115.5 h remaining (70.0%).
* M8a gate: 320 h of open work falls at or before it, of which 253.5 h is class X.
* M8b gate: a 90.5 h chain of open work running beside the software one, R1b through M8b.
* Critical chain through M8a: **its head is discharged**, S1 and every provenance decision being landed, and M1.9 with them, its memory plan closed on a population that decided every site, so what remains of it runs M1.2, M1.7, M3.5, M4.4, M5.3, M7.1 and M8a; independent Gallina statement authoring adds none, and M6.5a has left it the same way, its copy-based service landing as a statement artifact that wanted no backend. Over those items the chain sums to 72–155 h at a 113.5 h midpoint, M1.2 entering as its six open children because it carries no estimate cell of its own. The membership is the author's and the sum is arithmetic `tools/check.py` recomputes over those cells like the two gate figures above, so a re-price of anything on the chain moves it in the same edit, and it is read as a serial-path ceiling rather than as a bill.

### Sequencing

Dispatch by available inputs. The item-local **Start** and **Join** conditions own readiness; milestone numbers group acceptance evidence. Give contracts and repairs that release the compiler-to-boot path first claim on review and build capacity. Open an independent lane only when its write boundary, useful output and review slot are clear. A queue of finished-but-unreviewed work is a reason to finish integration before starting more.

With the fan-out having already run forty-four agent-parallel landings, the remaining scheduling constraint is the inputs and review capacity each new lane needs.

| Work package | Start and overlap | Integration boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Compiler representation and consumer contracts | Finish M1.2b's remaining acceptance work; review M1.2d's ABI and primitive contract and prepare M1.2f's harness alongside it | Land the representation once; g, c and e then implement in separate owned modules; d joins their outputs and f accepts the integrated backend |
| Firmware, kernel and storage | M3.5 and M4.4 author against the agreed entry/ABI contracts while the compiler runs; M5.3 reviews recovery and block-device semantics and authors policy-independent logic beside them | Each consumes the accepted backend and the real producer handoff its predicate names; M7.1 joins executable components |
| Composition and measurement | M7.1 settles the roster and assigns lowering of statement-only members early; M7.2/M7.3 prepare instrumentation together | Both analyses consume the same accepted roster, sharing captures where their workloads match; M8a consumes their distinct verdicts |
| Scalar RTL and platform | R1b curates datapath modules; R1c-ii prepares independent device wrappers against reviewed ports; R2 prepares trace/BMC harnesses; R3 prepares image loading | Whole-SoC and corpus checks use real core/device outputs; R3 joins M7.1's image; M8b need not wait for M7.2/M7.3 |
| Long-lead physical and board work | R5 prepares specimen/provenance protocols, M9a prepares activation analysis, M3.6b prepares timing observation, and M9 prepares board constraints | Physical qualification consumes measured parts and composition inputs; FPGA boot consumes R3. Modeled constants suffice only for the co-simulation claims |
| Bounded assessment and deferred host work | Use spare capacity for the open task's smallest decision or experiment, including independent document acts and contract/harness preparation | A decision or prototype releases only its named consumers; scope-cut proofs and new datapaths keep their own entry gates |

**Within every task, work in this order.** Identify the existing artifact and the unmet acceptance clause; resume partial work from its recorded revision. Review the narrow interface and a decisive positive/refusal case before implementation. Run the cheapest check that can reject the approach, then build one complete case before expanding over a generated population. Stop an optional experiment when its predeclared rejection criterion holds; price missing mandatory work at its owner rather than absorbing it or dropping the obligation. Reuse producer evidence only for the same source, configuration, assumptions and output identity. Finish with the task's required join and the integrator's landing wave.

**Shared work is paid and performed once.** M1.2f owns the compiler differential driver; component tasks add cases. M5.3 owns the modeled block-device contract, which R1c-ii also consumes. M7.1 owns the software image and boot recipe, R3 the RTL comparison, and Q10 the additional product judgment. Q2c owns missing-foundation assignments, Q21b the alternative-route comparison, and Q19c/Q20b share one held-out adaptation. Consumers return a defect to its producer instead of creating a parallel repair or replacement.

The summary's serial-path ceiling remains an accounting bound over its named cells. Exposing overlap changes neither estimates nor completion credit, and dividing effort by agent count is not a calendar forecast.

### Risks to the sequencing

What would falsify the order above, and what each risk is answered by. Nothing here is a hedge: every row names either the item that measures it or the property that bounds what it costs. **Where the item has run, the row states its answer rather than the question**, including where the answer came back adverse or found the row's own premise false, which is the only way a risk table stops being a list of things once worried about.

| Risk | Severity | What answers it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **The authority-class calibration rests on three completed items in the class that matters.** | High for the conclusion, low for the actions | Every act it motivates is independently justified. The convention already treats the ratio as a prior on range width rather than a re-price of midpoints, and the calibrated total is stated separately rather than folded in. **Who turns the prior into a measurement is answered, and the answer is adverse to the obvious repair**: S19 took the re-fit and ruled the two execution modes unpoolable, so the two class-X-authored items landed since, M6.0b and M6.3a, do not enter this pool, the count stays where it is, and what moves it is an attended class-X-authored landing rather than time. The re-fit also removed a reading this row had never claimed but is easily read into it: the class is not what makes an overrun, nine of the eleven attended overruns being class I, and what the pool of two says is how far over the two that authored ran. |
| **M1.1b forfeits SECOMP's integration path for robust preservation.** | High: that theorem is the §5 end-state | The arms and their forfeits are recorded at the decision. SECOMP's recomposition, blame and back-translation are stated over the compartment layer rather than over `cheririscV/`, so the pin stays for the front and middle end. Refusing the arm costs M1.2's re-price and no structure. |
| **M4.1b's read foreclosed the CHERI-seL4 start-from.** | Taken: M4.4, M6.3a and M6.5a are re-priced upward | Answered rather than pending. S7 recorded the read, 733 of 1,045 tracked files carrying a GPL-2.0-only tag with the CHERI work contributed under it, in the 6 h its four passes took together; M4.1b took the *author instead* arm at 0.4 h; and all three re-prices the severity column forecasts are taken, at M4.4, M6.3a and M6.5a. What is left exposed is the authoring itself, not an unread term. |
| **The M2 strike is wrong**, the multikernel wanting real SMP or the reference emulator being too slow. | High, for the whole of what was struck | Both rulings are taken: the multi-hart call at M4.4, and the throughput measurement at S4, which reads the emulator sufficient through M6 outright and past it against a boot budget M7.1 measures. Both upstreams stay gitlinks, so reinstating costs the pin. |
| **The Gallina front is authored against register acts that later move.** | Medium | Why S1 runs first. M4.2a is the precedent and the discipline: it authored what the register fixed and refused what it did not. |
| **Tier B admits a defect a full read would have caught.** | Medium | Tier B admits nothing without a rule holding whatever fact the item created, so the admissible defect class shrinks with every landing, and two of its four conditions are already gates that run, `run.py` deciding them on the machine the landing is taken on, while the absence of a Tier-A edit is a reading a person takes and K-84 holds the fourth as a declaration rather than as a promise. What an unattended runner would add is a second machine and a trigger, which S14 declined on a measurement. |
| **The model's own documentation bundle is not rich enough to supply what the four parsers read.** | Answered, and the answer is partial | S15 read it and both of the row's premises were false: the build had never produced the bundle on this host, and there were four live Sail parsers rather than five, `banks.py` having gone to the quarantine at S16. The bundle carries the definitions and the four parsers take them from it. **The retirement half does not follow and none of the seven rules retires**: each holds a figure sitting in hand-authored prose or in authored SystemVerilog, which a view of the Sail model is not a generator for, and that is a separate item at a separate estimate no cell carries. |
| **Generation makes a wrong fact consistent everywhere**, where a transcription would have shown a visible disagreement. | Medium | The tree's own evidence runs the other way. K-79's sites were held by nothing at all until the rule was written, and K-54's first run found a live disagreement in a document nobody had noticed. Consistency was never a property the transcriptions had; it was the property the rules were bolted on to create. |
| **The generator dividend does not materialize** across the Gallina front. | Measured, and adverse | The row named its own falsification condition and the condition was met: at M3.2, the first Gallina item to land with S13a and S13b in place, the QuickChick harness killed nothing and the enumerative vector grid killed nothing, all 244 kills being the prover's and the whole mutation score being the assumption gate refusing to compile. So the dividend is unrealized for a statement artifact, and what would change it is the widening M3.2 names and M4.3 has since performed once for the kernel surface: a `Require`, generator instances over the file's own types, and computable shadows of its deciders. It stays a small share of the reduction and is named per cell rather than asserted over the total. |
| **M6.2's offline-admission reading is wrong.** | Medium to high, and now only over M6.2b | S1 took the ruling: measured boot covers a statically composed image, which is what splits M6.2. The split is executed, M6.2a having landed the composition-time admission path at 12.4 h, so the residual risk stands over M6.2b alone, the on-device CIC kernel deferred behind the M8a gate, and a wrong reading costs that deferral rather than the work already done. |
| **The lanes exceed what one author can adjudicate**, Tier A being irreducible. | High for the calendar | Lane 1's queue is discharged, so the ceiling has moved off its serial content and onto the Tier-A read every landing still wants, which no instrument removes. The fan-out has run against that ceiling forty-four times and the landings held. Admit another lane when its inputs are fixed, its write boundary is exclusive, and review and build capacity are reserved. A growing review queue calls for finishing or reviewing existing work before opening more; the serial-chain sum determines no fixed lane count. |
| **Rupicola's target does not exist**, which is the harder half of what this row covers. | Answered, and the answer forks the route | Both halves are now measured at the artifact rather than read from the paper, and both readings moved. The **subset** blockers are not inductive datatypes and general recursion, which are at zero sites and three nullary enumerations respectively, but higher-order code, at 73 of M6.1a's 100 definitional commands. The **target** is a fork rather than an absence: the route stands up whole and has two exits, and neither carries both properties this plan claims for it. The verified exit emits 39 words of RV32I with no capability instruction, which is the plain-RISC-V target R-18-002 forbids by name; the reaching exit runs through a pretty-printer carrying no theorem, so its admissibility rides M1.2 completing the backend. The CompCert-C/VST route stays the default. |
| **The Sail-to-µSail translator's terms sit in its packaging rather than in a licence file.** | Answered, and the row's own premise was false | The premise was that it carries no licence at all, which a search over licence filenames and the forge established of licence *files* and not of terms. `default.nix`, `nanosail.nix` and `monads.nix` each state `licenses.bsd2` over a derivation whose `src` is the tree itself, and their three `pname` values are exactly the three packages it ships, so the grant is permissive and total and the automated translation is available. What is absent is the notice a redistributor retains, which a vendoring act composes from `dune-project`'s own authors field, and the hand translation is the fallback it always was rather than the expected path. [THIRD-PARTY.md](../../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries the reading and the rule that governs the next one. |
| **Quarantine rots the instruments S16 sets aside.** | Low to medium | They move whole, with their selftest cases, and K-83 holds that nothing outside the quarantine imports or launches one of the sources it carries. **The two contract documents did not move**: they stay in `docs/`, what was quarantined being the machinery and never the obligation, with K-70 and K-55 still reading both from the landing loop. |
| **The product thesis is unproved where the security thesis is strong**: a correct refusal can still be a product failure, and the performance headline states more than its own rows establish. | High for the product, low for the security architecture | Answered on the authored half and open on the measured one. Q1 has split the headline into five claims each naming its comparator and its evidence and authored [the product-gate contract](product-gate-contract.md), whose limits read columns no admission rule reads, so a candidate can fail the product gate while clearing every admission rule; every verdict there is undecided until an image exists, and its envelope values are proposed until the product's owner ratifies them. Q10 is the configuration at which useful behaviour, correspondence, resource accounting and physical feasibility have to meet, and it carries the scope revision if the mobile floor does not close on credible resources. |

### Considered and rejected

Recorded so they are not re-proposed, which is the same discipline [the alternatives register](../background/architectural-alternatives.md) keeps for the design.

- **Presentation feedback exposing another surface's completion time.** R-15-236c already forbids the observation, including through one surface's present callback. The line-period deadline belongs to the scanout reservation (R-11-008, R-15-230); compositor occupants are permuted at major-frame boundaries (R-11-023), and their bound is against the next reserved scanout (R-11-022a). Q17 opens no implementation work for this declined feedback.

- **Lowering as a post-pass over stock `ccomp -S` output.** Wrong on this machine's own semantics: an integer write yields an **untagged** capability (M0.6e e3, R-05-136), so ordinary RV64 pointer arithmetic clears the tag on every address it touches. Rewriting each such site into a capability increment needs type information CompCert has erased by `Asm`, and CompCert deliberately conflates pointers and integers at 64 bits. The idea's residue is what M1.1b rests on: the delta is concentrated in `Asmgen` and the calling convention.
- **Cutting the rules, the mutation selftest or the co-read ledger to reduce review cost.** Backwards. That machinery is exactly what makes a Tier-B landing admissible, so shrinking it moves work back into the one lane that cannot be widened.
- **Adopting CHERIoT-Ibex for the main core.** Already correctly excluded, its distinct compressed encoding being what R-15-005's single-encoding requirement refuses.
- **Parallelizing the build further.** I5, I6 and I10 measured it: clang took the generated unit's compile down by half and the residual is Sail's own single-threaded emission, which I6 showed a compiler mode cannot reach. CPU is not the constraint. Hardware still helps, through S9's finding class rather than through throughput.
- **Deleting `model/dependencies/`.** Checked at S3's scoping; every member has a live consumer, softfloat serving the surviving vector floating point and `asio` being S11's socket layer.
- **Raising the attended-hours budget.** Not a proposal at all. It is the one input this document cannot supply.
- **A second fast emulator, a whole-system simulator, or a new proof language** without a demonstrated blocker in the existing workflow. S4 measured that no loop before M7.1 is emulator-bound, and the two-language discipline this plan opens with is what a third language would spend.
- **Shipping extracted host code and treating its native execution as evidence** for the capability, timing and hardware theorem. The host processor and its runtime become dependencies of a different assurance argument, which is Q10's reading of a host product.
- **Feeding generated integer-pointer C into the capability compiler and taking source-to-device correspondence as inherited.** M1.6 measured the reaching exit's printer carrying no theorem, so the relation at that boundary is authored or it is absent, which is the audit Q2 carries.
- **Replacing an unqualified cell with a simulator parameter and calling the memory qualified.** R-15-247m wants the measured macro with its overheads, and a constant in Sail can be the name of an unsolved analog system; Ramulator2 and OpenRAM enter Q6 as comparators and neither qualifies the proposed stack.
- **Adding up improvements that address one bottleneck, or crediting vectorization, quantization and synthesis the baseline already assumes.** Q1 and Q8 keep each alternative in an estimate scenario of its own, and the whole-program bound on a partial speedup is stated with the Q items.
- **Installing every candidate dependency now.** One experiment is selected, its dependencies are reviewed and pinned, and it answers a question before the toolset widens; Q6's two simulators and Q5's solver are conditional on the comparison and the enumeration that precede them.

### S · Serial-path decisions and program instruments

The work that is undelegatable, the work that unblocks a lane, and the instruments the lanes run on. It sits ahead of the milestone track rather than inside one, because a milestone's items are the deliverable and these are the conditions under which more than one of them can be worked at once. Two rules sort it. **A decision is not an edit**: an item here that names an arm and its forfeits is closed by taking the arm, and nothing downstream is priced against it until it is. And **an instrument is authored before the lane that depends on it opens**, which is the acceptance-predicate convention above read forward rather than per item.

### Q · Assessment actions

The actions a reading of this design puts to the plan, carried as open items so that each is priced, sequenced and closed here rather than in a list beside it. [The critique's product assessment](../background/critique.md#the-two-theses) raises the first ten; the rest come from readings of the register against the outside world, and the provenance changes nothing about how an item is carried. Optional comparison items close by taking an arm, which may reject the candidate. Contract qualification can instead be an explicit input to an existing build item: Q22a supplies M4.4's revocation interface and may run beside compiler and firmware work. The individual dispatch clauses govern these dependencies; qualification of an interface does not claim its implementation or full proof. The later witness, attestation and physical claims wait for their own named evidence and are outside the M8a roster. Three readings sort the set. **Use more of the instruments already selected before selecting more.** Connecting a small generated implementation to the actual target, measuring a useful workload and checking the static resource assignment independently are the acts most likely to reduce effort without weakening the security architecture. **For capacity, start with less data and better placement, and change the physical topology only for a large physical gain.** Public immutable weights are the narrowest useful external-store experiment; more-transistor SRAM buys stability and voltage margin and not density; more matrix throughput does not lift a weight-bandwidth ceiling. **No figure in [the performance estimates](../performance/performance-estimates.md) rises because an item here is accepted.** Some of the repairs make those estimates less optimistic, and only a measured change against the current assumed baseline is credited, never a feature the estimates already assume.

**Assessment dispatch uses the remaining child and its unmet acceptance clauses.** Reuse landed contracts, instruments and review decisions; a completed setup or a reasoned rejection does not authorize commissioning its conditional implementation again.

Q14 and Q17 record the completed matrix and display/clipboard decisions. Q15 may start on the register and existing corpus. Q13's selection and host-build work needs no composed roster; its target performance comparison does. Q16 consumes the product limits, with proposed values still requiring the owner's decision. Q12 can read the candidate format before deciding whether another timing backend is worth qualifying.

Q19a and Q19b compare existing proof clients independently. Q2c and Q21a/Q21b supply the foundation ownership, core-design and compiler-comparison decisions; their reopening conditions still apply. The reviewed Q20a protocol is their common input. Q19c still needs checked concrete laws and target connections. Its qualified instance and frozen interface release Q20b's held-out adaptation; the same adaptation record completes both tasks' distinct judgments. Q20c adds final-artifact evidence after its producer inputs exist.

These trials add no prerequisite to the current M7.1 realization route. A failed candidate closes the corresponding decision where its predicate permits rejection; it does not block the boot path or silently retire a mandatory proof.

The component is a bounded wire-parser-shaped one, the consumer M1.6 re-pointed the route at, with an error path and explicit buffer bounds, and measurement decides which parser.
  * **Generate the imperative output and its checked functional relation**, keep the output reproducible rather than edited, and make one small source change and regenerate to expose the maintenance cost.

  * **Choose the device path explicitly**: capability-aware lowering through M1.2's backend, or a justified adaptation of the verified backend, and close the relation to the Sail model, the intermediate-language step included. A stock Bedrock2 RISC-V theorem is a theorem about another machine, and the Clight intermediate R-05-043 names is emitted by neither exit, so the route is a second front end until that bridge exists.

A host-only prototype completes the first experiment and not the device proof. The CompCert-C/VST route stays the default until a comparison displaces it, and the item stops where the bridge or the backend costs more than the duplication it removes. Higher-order specification code does not fit the target subset, which M1.6 measured over the supervision tree, so nothing here is sold as compilation of the golden model; functional compilation establishes neither constant-time behaviour, resource bounds, robust preservation nor the hardware refinement; no general Gallina compiler and no verified collector is built first; the domain-specific generation already in the plan stays where it fits, and hand-refined code stays where it makes the implementation and the proof simpler.

* [ ] **Q6 · Compare memory topologies on one workload** · 17 h, range 10–24 · 1.1% · X · after the M8a gate
  * **Execute:** Screen candidates first with the landed demand and placement evidence and available supply bounds. Reject infeasible candidates before simulator setup. Split traffic/interface accounting from physical-supply inquiry, then join on one workload. Reuse R4's measurand contract, pass the selected requirements to R5 and consume any already available measurements; this comparison does not wait for R5 qualification.
  * **Compare three candidates on the workload each actually satisfies**: a smaller qualified all-SRAM configuration, the two-class configuration R-15-247 states, and SRAM beside an explicit public-weight store.
  * Physical heterogeneity does not imply runtime migration. Any SRAM/DRAM comparator uses an explicit placement and controller policy; charge its refresh, row-buffer, disturbance and external-interface obligations without inventing a reactive placement mechanism. This clarifies the existing comparison and adds no fourth implementation project.
  * Physical-supply claims record the process, preparation, achieved optical resolution, resolved structures and complementary electrical evidence. An IRIS macro-level observation cannot establish individual bitcell or transistor correspondence against RTL, and evidence from a different process cannot qualify the proposed stack. This is assessment of supplied evidence; new characterization campaigns require separately priced work.
  * **For the public-weight candidate, stream bounded chunks authenticated against a trusted model identity**, keep authority-bearing state and the verification buffers local, import neither executable authority nor external capability tags, and specify buffering, the integrity-failure behaviour and the physical requester interface.
  * **Account for link bandwidth, verification throughput, scratch buffers, loading time, storage energy and visible access patterns.** Public weights do not make prompt-dependent sparse accesses public, and an external device can deny service, so no hard delivery is promised without a separately justified bound.
  * **Use supplied or measured physical coefficients**, ECC, tags where needed, periphery, routing, refresh, repair and thermal corners included, and ask a foundry or macro provider for realizable supply rather than literature cell density. R-15-247m wants the measured macro and R-17-063b books the stack as an offering of no foundry; R4 states the second class's architecture ahead of this comparison, as the definition of the candidate it weighs, and R5 is where the qualification evidence lands, which this comparison precedes.
  * **Decide the topology and the address-growth story while the format is undeployed.** R-15-007d freezes the width at the profile and stored authority is what makes the freeze permanent, so the decision is taken before a deployed population turns it into a migration; explicit block storage need not be mapped into the capability address space, and wider load and store addressing is a separate decision, a register act this item raises and does not take.
  * **Done when** the candidates are compared on useful workload, total resources, implementation maturity and physical threat claims, and the selected product has a credible supply path; a capacity-only winner that cannot deliver the required bandwidth loses. Ramulator2 serves a conventional-DRAM alternative and OpenRAM an SRAM macro exploration under a suitable technology setup, both conditionally: neither supplies or qualifies the proposed oxide-semiconductor stack, Ramulator's default out-of-order front end, cache and dynamic scheduling are not a model of this machine, so the trace and the controller policy are supplied, and an OpenRAM model in another process is not a scaled prediction of this one. The strongest route to much more physical storage changes the one-die realization (R-15-162) and its absence of an external memory bus, can expand accessible model data without widening directly addressable RAM, and may make a larger model possible while making each token slower.
* [ ] **Q7 · Qualify remanence and key lifetime across both memory classes** · 14 h, range 8–20 · 0.9% · X · after the M8a gate
  * **Execute:** Take the deployment threat decision first; a supported refusal avoids building an unused protection subsystem. If protection is required, compare the cheapest admissible local-storage or retention reduction before encryption. Share Q6's physical/interface inputs and assign any new construction before implementation.
  * **Decide whether abrupt-power-loss confidentiality for sensitive bulk is required for the intended deployment.** R-17-058f books the second class's remanence as a residual no construction closes, and it does not disappear behind capability-tag invalidation, which retires stale authority and not plaintext. The threat decision can be taken ahead of the rest of this item.
  * **Qualify the first-class and key-storage premise as well.** R-15-200's cold-boot claim needs evidence for the actual implementation under abrupt loss, cooling, supply discharge and retained power states. Distinguish a minimum retention time needed for reliable operation from a maximum recoverability window used for confidentiality. Orderly zeroization and the word SRAM establish neither that window nor that an ephemeral key disappears before bulk data.
  * **If it is required, compare three arms**: keeping sensitive data in protected local storage, reducing the retained sensitive bulk, and an encrypted bulk path whose ephemeral key has an independently justified lifetime and destruction mechanism, R-17-059a scoping the attacker who reaches the plaintext after the key is gone.
  * **For an adversary-controlled memory interface, include authentication, freshness, address and generation binding, and atomic data-and-metadata updates**, from a defined standard construction and never a new cipher or mode.
  * **Measure** masked cryptographic throughput, metadata storage, boot and power-loss behaviour and transition latency, and keep ciphertext confidentiality apart from the access-pattern, power, live-probe and denial-of-service risks that remain.
  * **Estimate boundary.** The range includes threat-window specification and assessment of supplied first-class, key-storage and bulk evidence. R5 and R5a own qualification of the separate device measurements; new laboratory campaigns, erase circuits or memory cryptography require separately priced implementation work. Source qualification and the threat decision may start before M8a; the physical verdict cannot precede the required evidence.
  * **Done when** the design either has a concrete protection construction with evidence for its threat window, or the product declines the deployments needing that property. The first arm changes R-15-199, the memory path carrying no cryptography, and is a register act; the second is scope control and not hardening. The existing cryptographic proof and oracle work comes first, and a verified primitive or a masked AES block is not a verified memory-protection subsystem. The gain is a defined physical threat window closed at more area, metadata, effort and latency, with any performance benefit indirect, such as a larger conventional medium becoming acceptable.

* [ ] **Q12 · Re-derive the inference floor against a ternary weight format and state its prompt-processing term** · 10 h, range 6–14 · 0.6% · X
  * **Execute:** Run the static file/header predicates first using the existing demand-report tooling. Qualify another timing backend only if its incremental measurement is worth the setup. Prepare the prompt-processing criterion alongside the static read, then join quality and target evidence without crediting a host rate.
  * **Re-run the static predicates over a natively ternary weight file, restating each predicate rather than only its number.** Three of the four are [the demand report](../performance/inference-demand.md)'s section 5: weight bytes, bytes read per generated token, and KV bytes per token in two cache formats. The fourth, the resident buffers the loader allocates at an 8,192-token context, is its section 6, so a re-derivation aimed at section 5 alone reproduces three of the four and misses that the fourth is stated per cache format. Section 3's licence and publisher-conversion discipline is re-run on the candidate at the candidate's own revision, and the file's digest is checked against the publisher's tree listing before it is loaded, exactly as that section does for the weight it chose.
  * **Take the multiple between two measured whole-file densities and never between two nominal widths.** The format R-18-004a(vii) calls four-bit costs more than four bits per parameter once its block scales and its wider embedding and down-projections are counted, which section 5 measures and section 8's projection carries; a shipped ternary file likewise carries a shared scale per block of weights, so its whole-file density is above the nominal figure its own literature quotes. A multiple taken between a nominal four bits and a nominal 1.58 is two errors that cancel, and it is not the move on the term the report says binds.
  * **Carry no from-scratch constraint.** Converting a trained full-precision model to native ternary weights after the fact is a published pipeline (BitNet Distillation, arXiv:2510.13998) rather than an open problem, and the candidate below declares as its base the model the report already measures, so an item recording quantization-aware training from scratch as an honest constraint would put a false claim into the register on the day it landed.
  * **The candidate, and what disqualifies the obvious one.** `prism-ml/Ternary-Bonsai-4B-gguf` is a natively ternary model at or above the size the member states, published with its own GGUF conversion and declaring `Qwen/Qwen3-4B` as its base, which is the base section 3 selected, so section 5's table and section 7's comparator re-run against a directly comparable file rather than against a different model; its terms are read at its own revision under section 3's discipline before it is loaded, and that reading is what admits or refuses it. Microsoft's BitNet b1.58, the one ternary weight format [the alternatives survey](../background/architectural-alternatives.md) cites, passes that discipline on both limbs and fails on size alone, below the parameter count the member states. That survey cites it as the concession half of an entry whose disposition already records the weight-format lever as adopted, so what this item adds is not the mechanism but the arithmetic no artifact has taken.
  * **Split the measurement by what the pinned instrument reaches.** The static predicates are read from the file's own header by a tool section 2's pin already builds, so the bytes half needs no new instrument and is the load-bearing half. A timed row does: the candidate's block type is carried by a vendor fork and not by the pin, which is a second instrument pin, a second [THIRD-PARTY.md](../../THIRD-PARTY.md) row with its terms read at that pin, and the first file under [inference-demand/](../performance/inference-demand/) whose build pair differs from every other one there. The timed half waits on that pin being judged worth taking, and the item may decline it and say so.
  * **Score the result against R-18-004b's second-class capacity and bandwidth comparisons, beside the rows already there, and name the register act that follows.** R-18-004a(vii) states the member's format with the same grammatical force as it states its parameter count, so a ternary member is an amendment to that entry measured against the same recorded floor (R-18-004d) and never a re-scoring against it, and R-18-004d's versioned demonstration set names the member it amends. The alternatives survey's ternary entry records the weight-format lever as adopted at no specification surface, and an amendment to R-18-004a(vii) is specification surface, so that disposition moves in the same act or the two disagree. What the projection over the published file sizes says the measurement will find is a weight stream inside the grant floor R-18-004b states, which no configuration in the report reaches; that is a demand fitting the stated minimum and not a ground for moving it, R-18-004b's figures being floors on supply and not caps on demand, as section 8 already says of them.
  * **State a prompt-processing term where the register states only a sustained generation rate.** R-18-004a(vii) floors a generating rate over a declared context, and R-12-085's declared ceiling names the resident bytes, the context length, the quantization formats, the expert count and top-k, the KV footprint per token and the bank grant, so a composition meeting every one of them and answering its first token arbitrarily late passes both. The distinction itself is not missing: [the estimates](../performance/performance-estimates.md) already carry prompt processing and token generation as two claim rows with two comparators, and the report measures the two separately while declining to carry any host rate into the target. What is missing is a quantity in a normative entry, and R-18-004a's own criterion makes a member carrying no quantity a candidate can miss a review-gate finding, so the term is stated as a quantity or it is not stated at all. *Decode* is not the word for it here, R-18-004b's aggregate reserving that word for the video frame pool; the two phases are prompt processing and token generation, which the outside literature calls prefill and decode.
  * **The grant stays single, and only the admitted rate can be stated per phase.** R-15-247p whole-binds banks to islands and lets no bank count vary with occupancy or load, and R-15-188 has the shared resources, the NoC, the memory controller and main memory, never scale, so a bank grant differing between the two phases either moves banks across an island boundary, which R-15-247p refuses, or names no quantity at all, the island holding the larger of the two at every instant either way. The register's own shape for two enumerated modes of unequal demand is R-15-236b's and R-15-238c's: admit at the binding point and let the lighter one run inside it as slack. This item therefore takes no per-phase grant, and a proposal for one is an R-15-247p amendment reaching [the bank-count contract](../hardware/bank-count-dse-contract.md) and the rule that holds it, which is a different item and a different price.
  * **A two-valued operating point is admissible in one shape, and the item names that shape before proposing any.** R-11-017 has the admission proof select each partition's operating point and emit the assignment as one artifact, and R-15-188 makes that point a composition-time constant switched only at partition boundaries, so the two phases carry two operating points only as two admitted partitions on the composition-fixed wheel, each with its own constant. One partition whose point is chosen at a data-dependent transition is the load-following [the absence contract](../hardware/absence-contract.md)'s A-12 excludes by its evidence column, whose admitted inputs are the composed assignment and the mode index alone; and a global mode change per phase is what R-11-018 and R-15-189 admit only as rare, RoT-attested and on explicit authority, which a transition driven by prompt length is not, R-11-019 being the register's own statement of why an interaction-rate transition is not that axis. The admissible shape costs no new mechanism and is not free: R-15-220 already carries operating-point relock as a switch term and R-11-009 counts the switch-duty ratio explicitly, so the switch constant and the visit rate both move and R-11-011's pinning threshold is where that lands. Whether to take the shape is a decision this item states and does not take, the energy it buys reaching the compute island alone because the memory side never scales.
  * **This is neither Q4a re-opened nor Q4b brought forward.** Q4a is landed and its note is frozen, so what its report concludes is amended by a record beside it rather than by a growth of that item's asks; Q4b is the target-kernel half behind the M8a gate, and nothing here measures a target, this item's budget being derived from bytes, the register's own rate and the register's own supply floors exactly as Q4a's report derives it. What this item adds to Q4a is a weight format its ladder does not contain and a term its verdict does not carry; what it leaves to Q4b is every timed target figure.
  * **The measurement is owed and it is runnable today, and it is taken when this item runs rather than now.** Nothing in the static half waits on an image, a kernel or a bank grant: the candidate is published, its terms are readable at its own revision, and the header read runs on the instrument section 2 already pins. The item is the act that takes it, and no figure from it is quoted anywhere until it has been.
  * **Done when** the ternary configuration's static demand is recorded under [inference-demand/](../performance/inference-demand/) on the report's own predicates and scored against R-18-004b's second-class capacity and bandwidth comparisons beside the four-bit rows already there; the register act the result implies is either taken or stated with what it costs and what else moves with it; and R-18-004a(vii) and R-12-085 either carry a prompt-processing quantity a candidate can miss or the item records why none is writable and names what owes it. Quality is not decided here, the comparator being section 7's and the threshold Q1's, and a narrower weight buys nothing until that threshold admits it. A projection is not a measurement, a host figure is not a target figure, and neither half of this item lowers a floor.
  * **Acceptance predicate at entry, in two halves, and only the first is writable today.** The first half closes when a static record for the ternary file sits under [inference-demand/](../performance/inference-demand/) produced by the same predicate the four-bit rows were produced by, its digest checked against the publisher's tree listing at a named revision before the file was loaded, and its budget columns computed by the same arithmetic as the rows beside it; a row whose predicate differs from the rows it sits beside fails this rather than extending the table. The second half's predicate is that R-18-004a(vii) or R-12-085 names a prompt-processing quantity in the unit of the clause it feeds, with R-18-004d's set stating the member's pass condition in that unit. **That predicate may not be writable at this item**, and the refusal is recorded here on M5.3's precedent where it is not: no artifact measures a target prompt-processing rate, the report's own rule forbids carrying a host one into the target, and the earliest artifact that could supply one is Q4b's behind the M8a gate. A quantity taken from the host to satisfy the predicate fails it.
* [ ] **Q13 · Pin the bring-up Wasm interpreter and re-read its ranking against the absent predictors** · 14.5 h, range 9–20 · 0.9% · X
  * **Execute:** Perform the existing-artifact selection, licence/pin and host-build review without waiting for a composed roster. Reuse the current Wasm clients to expose unsupported behavior early; defer target ranking to the actual R3/Q8 measurements rather than building another full-system emulator.
  * **Separate host confinement from guest object safety.** Core Wasm remains the baseline. Use a bounded adjacent-object overflow and stale-pointer example to show what arena confinement permits internally; if a real consumer requires finer protection, compare safe-source compilation, module separation and MSWasm-shaped handles against the same host-call and resource contract. Record a reasoned refusal if no such consumer exists. The expanded range covers the boundary examples and candidate assessment, not implementing a second dialect or proving its semantics.
  * The semantics, interpreter and confinement theorems must name compatible exact pins and supported subsets before curation savings are credited. SpecTec's shared-source generation alone supplies no interpreter theorem. A renamed source retaining its canonical identity must still reject an incorrect cached executable through the existing final-artifact evidence; a correct source hash supplies no cache-correctness proof.
  * **Pin a release where the paragraph names only a lineage.** [The porting guide's Servo entry](userspace-porting.md#servo-the-contained-browser-engine) names a wasmi-lineage pure-Rust interpreter standing in as the unverified bring-up interim and names no release, and no register entry names that interim at all, so this is the first version pin that paragraph takes rather than a bump. R-14-013a is the engine the interim stands in front of and not the interim itself, and the pin is recorded where the interim is defined. Say in the same act which of the adopted upstream changes are expected to survive curation into that engine and which are interim-only, because each of them is surface the R-14-013a refinement against R-14-013b's pinned semantics eventually covers.
  * **Two of the adopted changes are already in tension with the register and are booked before they are taken.** Function bodies held in append-only buckets that never move for the engine's lifetime buy baked-in body pointers by never releasing, and R-14-015 wants a declared selection and release algorithm inside a composition-sized arena under the R-08-046 bounded-pool contract; adopting the design into the interim and discovering at curation that it cannot be carried is the expensive version of this item. The upstream's artifact-size knob that disables guest validation carries the precondition that the embedder controls its inputs, which is the opposite of the case R-14-013a exists for, untrusted dynamic content under robust guest confinement, so it is admitted only where the module is image-resident and admitted and never on the R-14-008a delivery path. Neither is adopted outright.
  * **The branch-site inversion is a measurement-correctness matter and not a speed one.** Upstream's single largest gain comes from un-collapsing a `csel` back into two branch sites, undoing what `DestinationPropagation`, a Rust 1.92 MIR pass, had merged: CoreMark rises from about 2800 to over 4200 on an Apple M2 Pro, which [the engine's 2.0 release post](https://wasmi-labs.github.io/blog/posts/wasmi-v2.0/) states as its most important single change. The mechanism is a dynamic predictor getting two entries with separable histories instead of one entry with mixed history. Rows A-04, A-05 and A-06 of [the absence contract](../hardware/absence-contract.md) remove the direction predictor, the target predictor and the return-address stack, prediction being static-only with zero mutable predictor state, so the structure the gain rests on does not exist here and the upstream fix is not a fix on this die. Letting the MIR pass collapse the two paths is the cheaper act, and it is a build the item takes rather than a revert it authors.
  * **The porting note that generalizes it, and it extends a sentence the register already carries.** Any interpreter result that improves by feeding a branch predictor is measuring hardware this machine does not have, so the whole upstream benchmark ranking is re-read entry by entry under A-04, A-05 and A-06 before any of it informs a figure, and an entry whose gain rests on predictor, target-predictor or return-stack behaviour is recorded as unread on this target rather than carried across. R-14-015's Accept already states the narrow case, a dispatch loop on a machine with static branch prediction and no instruction cache being the worst tenant a bulk fetch constant could have, so the note is that sentence generalized from the arena to the ranking. The same reading is owed of the two changes that trade dependent chains for offsets, the flattened instance layout and the baked-in body pointers, whose value on a machine with no data cache and no prefetcher (A-07, A-09, A-10) is expected to be larger here than upstream measures and for which upstream reports no isolated figure at all.
  * **Narrow R-14-008a rather than reopen it: what is undecided is direct versus indirect threading.** That entry already admits threaded dispatch by name as one of its ahead-of-time levers, so the open question is only the form. Upstream reports indirect-threaded roughly 10 to 15 percent slower than direct-threaded and significantly smaller in the memory its IR occupies, which is a trade against R-14-008b's footprint budget rather than against a policy. Direct threading makes the IR a buffer of code pointers, and that stays inside the deleted class for a structural reason rather than a judgment: the buffer is data, so it consumes no executable SRAM budget, which is the capacity measurement the static-code-overlay trigger is stated as, and no component fills an executable bank at run time, which is the runtime write-then-execute consumer R-15-047's exclusion rests on. **A threading scheme that inlines handler bodies rather than pointers is a different mechanism and is already refused**, R-14-008b's Accept admitting no set on the ground that it is data rather than generated code. Direct threading's own unpriced cost sits in the typed-assembly derivation and it is narrower than it first looks: R-05-112 already contemplates sentries loaded from vtables and R-05-113 already requires an indirect transfer's code type to carry the finite set of labels its reachable sentries target, so the mechanism and its typing obligation both exist; what has no rule is a `code`-typed field at a slot inside a runtime-written buffer, [the typed assembly language](../languages/typed-assembly-language.md) typing a register file at block entry and carrying no general memory typing former. Defaulting to indirect defers that addition whole, which is the argument for taking it as the default until the typing is priced.
  * **Two register acts this item raises and does not take.** **R-14-013b declares the guest semantics version-frozen and names no version**, and no Wasm version number appears anywhere in the tracked corpus, so the freeze has no frontier written down; the act is to name the frozen version and record Wasm 3.0 as declined rather than not-yet-reached. The version is the tighter instrument than a proposal list, Wasm 3.0 being a language version whose additions reach past the typed references, exception handling and garbage collection an engine roadmap names, and it is written *Wasm 3.0* so that it is never read as the engine's own release number. Its garbage-collection half needs no new ground: R-14-015 wants fixed object-size classes or a statically proved bounded representation, a maximum live object count per class, a declared selection and release algorithm and, where guest-level collection exists, a complete WCET and non-interference model, none of which Wasm GC as specified supplies, and its Accept names the collector shapes nothing here may rely on. **The deterministic profile is the one named exception the decline carries**, and it narrows exactly one thing, the choice of NaN payload inside R-14-013b's pinned numeric semantics: its relaxed-vector half has nothing to act on, R-14-013d admitting fixed-width SIMD only, and it delivers no blanket determinism, memory and table growth staying nondeterministic by design. It is booked as a pinned-semantics amendment **and** as a build-configuration fact, R-14-013a's Accept requiring both theorems to check against the pinned semantics at build, so an engine built without the feature would not refine an amended pin. R-14-013d's curation rule gates it and the question is asked before the amendment is drafted: the semantics enters the pin from the tracked mechanized upstream rather than by authorship, so whether that lineage reaches the profile decides whether the exception is writable. **Neither act moves the §16 replay record**, whose members are drawn values crossing an interface and not a guest language's choices.
  * **Done when** the porting paragraph names the pinned upstream release and says which adopted changes are expected to survive curation, the threading form is chosen with its reason recorded, the ranking is re-read entry by entry with the predictor-fed entries marked unread on this target, and each register act above is either taken at its entry or declined with a reason. A faster interim is not the payoff and nothing here is a credit against the no-JIT cost R-14-008f books as accepted; what the item buys is a ranking that means something on this machine and an interim whose design does not have to be undone at curation. Adopting an upstream change into the unverified interim is not an admission of it into the R-14-013a engine, and this item takes no step toward the memory typing the direct-threaded form would want.
  * **Acceptance predicate at the documents and the host, and a stated gate at the machine.**
  * **At the documents and the host the predicate is three readings and one build.** (i) Every entry in the upstream benchmark suite carries one of two verdicts, *reads on this target* or *predictor-fed and unread here*, the second naming the structure the gain rests on, and the `csel` entry is of the second kind. (ii) A case-insensitive search of [docs/](..) for the engine's name returns the porting paragraph, this item and its note, and nothing inside [the estimates](../performance/performance-estimates.md), which carries no figure from this upstream and no CoreMark figure at all until the gate below is passed. (iii) Two host builds of the dispatch handler, one as the MIR pass leaves it and one with the branch sites separated, differ at their dispatch sites, which is the difference the machine half would measure; a host cycle count over either is recorded as a host figure and carried into no estimate.
  * **At the machine there is no predicate, and the reason is that no item before R3 puts a composed image on the elaborated core.** The configuration the question is asked of is the one whose predictor parameters [the provenance record](../../rtl/synthesis-provenance.md) binds to A-04, A-05 and A-06, and the cycle separation between the two dispatch forms is the profiling Q8 opens over the image R3 boots. R-15-101 makes the comparison well posed there, cycle-level timing being a function of the instruction stream and architectural state alone, so the two forms differ by a decidable property of the stream rather than by learned history; what that is worth in cycles is Q8's to report and not this item's to assert. Until it is reported no figure from this upstream enters [the estimates](../performance/performance-estimates.md), which is the whole of what the guard above holds.

* [ ] **Q15 · Weigh the contactless proximity surface and take an arm on each of its four uses** · 16 h, range 9–23 · 1.0% · X
  * **Execute:** Decide the use cases before selecting a radio or protocol implementation. Independent use-case readings may proceed together; one integrator reconciles their shared band, grammar and authority decisions. A refusal closes its decision branch without opening the associated subsystem work.
  * **The radio question is three entries and a price, and only one of the three is a cell fill.** R-15-122 is the cheap one: its acceptance clause has the buffer, the channel/frequency word and the event schedule loaded by the §12 control plane before the event, so a fixed frame-delay schedule is a value the admitted sequencer already takes and the block still makes no protocol decision. The other two are not cell fills. R-15-124 enumerates PA, LNA, filters, switches and antenna tuners, and a magnetically coupled loop with load modulation is none of the five, so admitting one is an enumeration amendment carrying a criterion of its own rather than a member of a bank already described; R-15-125 states the secondary layer as a TX power and spectrum-mask limit, which is not the form a near-field strength limit takes; and R-15-121's register-slave datapath with its digital front end is where the coding would sit. R-15-123's no-foreign-computers line decides against every commodity part in this class, which runs its stack as firmware on a hidden core, so what is weighed is verified RTL or nothing. **The endpoint is priced where the register prices it, and that is not at `fence.t`.** R-15-015b keeps a device-space store out of the store buffer so that R-15-218's padded per-class constant is not set by the slowest endpoint's accept latency, and it is there that R-12-046's divided card clock is named the on-die existence proof that such an endpoint is reachable at all. A second slow endpoint therefore sets the bound R-15-015c states, device authority provisioned by latency class in the attested devicetree (R-09-007), and R-15-015d is what decides whether it earns a thin driver compartment or spends a divisor for nothing.
  * **Card emulation is a second instance of a discipline the register already carries, and the item says which half is new.** R-12-047 already requires APDU/TPDU traffic to be parsed only in software by a Narcissus-derived verified copy-once reader in a zero-authority compartment, so the parser obligation is not new work and is cited rather than restated; R-12-046 is the contact-side precedent for the silicon half, a fixed-function interface block that moves bytes and interprets nothing with no DMA and no APDU semantics, and it is the eUICC's own entry rather than a general one, so a contactless block is a new entry with its own criterion. What is genuinely new is the compartment that answers as a card, which runs the discipline in the opposite direction to the AKA client's, and R-12-084a with R-15-238d is the containment footing it takes: a compartment holding one session's ring and nothing else, minting nothing, naming no device, and faulting as a contained crash. The arm is taken by stating that compartment or refusing it, not by re-proposing the parser.
  * **The payment arm is a declination booked as a coverage trade, and it moves two lock-state entries rather than only citing them.** EMVCo issues type approval per product at Level 1 and Level 2, a live network token requires a registered token-requestor relationship with the scheme, and host card emulation removes the secure element without removing that relationship, so the credential is gated by scheme membership rather than by anything this platform can verify; some of the governing documents are membership-gated or licensed, which is a fact about the reading and not a defect in it. The trade is written in the shape R-17-051 already uses for the generation floor, a coverage-for-security cost stated rather than hidden, and R-04-011 is what refuses reading a second tolerated foreign computer out of the first, the eUICC being an exception and not a pass. The two entries that move are R-15-146, whose enable is driven by grant liveness and carries an in-use indication no compromised component can suppress, which a part answering an RF field while the host is off holds nothing to satisfy, and R-15-147, whose cut set is the microphone, the camera and the USB data lanes with the radio staying page-reachable because the paging task holds its capability: a power-reserve element would be the first candidate in that set with no capability holder to justify a carve-out. [The inspirations record](../background/inspirations.md) already carries the field evidence that a certified element fails in production, so the declination cites it rather than arguing the case fresh.
  * **The credential uses separate by whose gate each carries, and the role question is taken first because it is the only one that is not a reading.** WebAuthn classifies an authenticator reached over NFC as roaming, which is the word R-12-020 declines and the word [the alternatives record](../background/architectural-alternatives.md) uses when it names the on-die path the platform-authenticator role, so the item decides whether R-12-020's ground governs, an external device being a foreign computer running unverified firmware, or its role word does, and reports the answer instead of assuming the ground travels; Q9's confined external-authenticator client is the same entry read from the other side and the two are decided together. The rest are dispositions over outside documents: ISO 18013-5 device retrieval needs no scheme membership, though a verifier still needs the issuing authority's trust anchors and the standard itself is a paid document; the NFC Forum tag and message specifications are openly specified and publicly obtainable for a fee, which is the cheapest arm to admit and not a free one; PCI MPoC is a certification gate whose currency is attestation rather than a proprietary credential relationship, which is a statement about the gate and not a claim of fit; and the W3C Digital Credentials API is a Working Draft that specifies no proximity transport, so it is adjacent to this question rather than part of it.
  * **Done when** each of the four arms is taken and recorded where the arm belongs: an amendment or a refusal at the §15 radio entries, each amendment carrying a criterion that can fail; a compartment entry or a refusal at §12; a stated trade at §17 with the two §15 lock-state entries either amended or the whole option declined; and a per-use disposition for the credential surfaces. Declining the entire surface completes this item, a decision item closing by taking an arm. What does not complete it is an arm left open because a governing document is membership-gated, which is recorded as the reason it could not be read rather than as a pending read. Nothing here is a performance or capacity act, and the reading establishes no coverage: an approval regime read is not an approval held, and no arm makes this platform a payment-scheme participant.
  * **Acceptance predicate, stated over the register rather than over the report, because a decision item's deliverable is the arm it takes.** Four conditions, each decidable by reading the tree. Every arm taken as an amendment carries a criterion line that decides on a physical or structural property, so an entry admitting a near-field path names what a criterion can fail on and does not restate R-15-124's existing clause. Every arm taken as a refusal is written where this corpus already writes refusals, [the alternatives record](../background/architectural-alternatives.md), with its honest cost booked as R-12-020's entry books portability. The endpoint-latency sentence names R-15-015c and R-09-007 and does not name `fence.t`, R-15-015b excluding the device store from the buffer precisely so that the padded constant is not that endpoint's. And no sentence in any of the four arms asserts that the smartcard vocabulary is absent from this corpus: the contact-side interface block and its verified-parser pattern are present and normative at R-12-045, R-12-046 and R-12-047, and it is the near-field path and the payment credential layer that are unconsidered. Every co-read pair the arms move is read and blessed rather than left standing, which K-61 reports and no `--fix` clears.
