* [ ] **Q19 · Qualify existing proof tooling and one reusable component**
  * **The current spec owns acceptance, not the language proposal.** R-05-017, R-05-018a and R-05-018b govern these experiments; [the strategy's tooling map](../languages/verification-strategy.md#existing-tooling-and-implementation-owners) records candidate capabilities and limits. The estimates below are bounded attended agent-session judgments, not conversions of that document's person-week research budgets and not measured savings. Each child can reject a candidate with a reproducible failing comparison; that decision does not claim the rejected tool's functionality or complete another child's gate.
  * **Read existing embedded tools within the design comparison, not as an installation roster.** AddressC and LiveVerif are the first proof-surface references; SuSLik's Iris/VST certificates are the first synthesis reference. Velvet/Loom/LeetProof inform interaction and specification testing, while CFML/Sisyphus inform the maintenance comparison. These readings stay within the children's existing scope and estimates; reproducing another toolchain, porting a certifier or building a proof-repair service requires separate pricing before work opens. FP2 and creditmonad remain resource-design references, MiniC is unmaintained, and Perennial belongs to the filesystem work already selected.
  * **Select the closest existing mechanism before implementing a new one.** The [wider comparison](../languages/verification-strategy.md#closest-matches-to-the-complete-workflow) makes DeepSEA and Sepref references for representation and refinement, and the [Rocq parametric theory](../languages/verification-strategy.md#capability-aware-and-parametric-verification) a reference for interface laws. Choose the part relevant to the bounded client; this is not a requirement to install or reproduce every survey entry. Verified Dafny/CakeML and the Bedrock2/Kami example are evidence-endpoint comparators. Rocqet, Pyrosome, Heapster, Crane and the EVM compiler work remain research references without implementation cells.
  * **Qualify before incorporating.** Select immutable revisions compatible with the actual Rocq, stdlib, stdpp and plugin tuple, read the upstream licences and dependency notices, and record any incorporation in [THIRD-PARTY.md](../../THIRD-PARTY.md). A compatibility probe uses a disposable or separate switch and never silently downgrades the proof switch. Recurring commands enter through `tools/run.py`, with guest dependencies in WSL; existing host-only gates remain independent of them. Q6's conventional-memory simulators are conditional on a topology comparison needing them, Q5's solver on enumeration exposing a need, and Q19's automation on a demonstrated proof-client cost. A small decisive rejection ends an experiment before a new toolchain or subsystem is built. Mandatory obligations survive a rejected route and return to their named owners.

No assessment completion automatically increases a performance estimate. For an optimization affecting fraction `f` of runtime by factor `s`, the unchanged-cost bound is `1 / ((1 - f) + f / s)`; added transfers or changed scheduling require a fresh whole-workload comparison. Better packing is not more fabricated capacity, and host performance is not target throughput.

The matrix extension is not one of those rows *yet*, and which act admits it is now written down: R-15-014a's second-act delta carries it at **(ix)**, its instruction surface, operand sourcing and encoding together, conditioned on R-15-116's sustained dense-GEMM margin, which no backend and no composed image can supply before M1. So custom-1 and custom-3 stay whole against it exactly as M0.6g and M0.6h left them whole for the rows the freeze's single measured act admits, and spending either here would be modelling a measurement-conditioned provisional row, which is the one thing M0.6g's exit condition forbids.

Closing it needs the profile to enumerate the extension's instructions the way it enumerates every other bespoke row's, which is a register and profile act ahead of a model one (R-15-001a).
    * **The margin the surface is conditioned on now has a normative denominator, and that is the one thing about this item that could still close it the other way.** R-15-116a fixes the RVV expression the margin is taken against as the strongest one the profile admits on the M class, dot products included wherever the standing adoption R-15-067l records has ratified by then, and requires the report to record the denominator's own extension set beside its figure. A batched dot product collects the reduction tree an int8 GEMM otherwise pays per output element and produces eight outputs at once, so it is a term in that denominator rather than in the numerator, and *the admission above was taken against a denominator this profile has since made stronger*. Both branches are live and this item closes on either. If the margin clears, the disposition is as written and the surface is a pin or an authored encoding. If it falls below the threshold, R-15-116 **deletes the extension by its own criterion**, and this item closes by striking its instruction half rather than by authoring one: `vmclear`'s empty matrix inventory (below) becomes empty by construction, R-15-117's software duty and R-15-009's re-pin obligation go with the unit, and custom-1 and custom-3 stay whole for something else. Under §1's value function that is the cheaper outcome, which is why the measurement is worth taking before the second act rather than after it.
    * **The re-pin compares all three tracks.** R-15-009 names attached AME (`Ztt`), integrated IME (`Zvvm`) and vector-coupled VME (`Zvt`); [the profile's recorded readings](../hardware/isa-profile.md#3-custom-and-fork-and-frozen-instructions) identify their source revisions, and the [matrix comparison](../background/architectural-alternatives.md#x86-acearm-sme-architectural-state-not-imported) records their state and switch costs. The re-pin re-reads each candidate's plan and source revision, inventories registers and CSRs, complete zeroize scope, memory and restart behavior, dependence on M-class VLEN and the declared systolic geometry, and arithmetic, rounding and scaling semantics. No track is selected by its family name or vector-file reuse. R-15-116a's strongest-RVV-denominator measurement comes first: a failing margin deletes the extension, while a passing margin leaves R-15-014a (ix) to admit its instruction surface, operand sourcing and encoding through the existing review gate. Available independent oracle coverage is a term in that comparison, not a separate admission criterion.

    * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **65 properties against 62**, three of them new; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **270 keys, unchanged**, the region class being a property of a list member rather than a key of the configuration tree; the differential corpus green at 21 of 21 with every digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green and the tree unmoved at 4,466 bytes, no configuration here declaring a scratchpad for it to carry; each of the **eight** new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on all 64 rules with every mutant killed; and both type checkers clean.
    * Ten findings.
      * **The profile books the matrix extension as a unit and names no instruction, so there is nothing here to admit.** Every other bespoke row in [isa-profile.md](../hardware/isa-profile.md) §3 names its instruction, operand form and encoding decision; the matrix row names a unit, a disposition and a re-pin target, and a construct the enumerations do not name is absent (R-15-007j). Three neighbouring decisions are taken and read as though they were this one: no architectural tile file, no block-scale register (R-15-117 keeping de-quantization in software), and outer-product sourcing recorded as an exploration question rather than an ISA commitment. All three are about what the extension does not **hold**, and a surface whose operands come from vector registers and memory needs none of it. What is left undecided is the mnemonic, the operand encoding and the opcode.
      * **The empty profile cell was the symptom and the missing sorting rule was the defect.** No Sail file carries a matrix instruction; what [core/core_class.sail](../../model/model/core/core_class.sail) holds is geometry, a composition declaring how large a systolic array is rather than a machine knowing what it executes. What sorts the surface is R-15-116's admission against a **sustained dense-GEMM margin**, a measurement against generated output, so the surface belongs to the second act at (ix) rather than to an amendment. R-15-014b now states the missing rule: a unit booked without an instruction surface names the act that will book it, and one booked with neither is a review-gate finding. **So this half is out of scope rather than blocked**, and modelling the surface now would be the measurement-conditioned provisional row M0.6g's exit condition refuses.
      * **A scratchpad is a placement rather than a structure, which decides how much of one a Sail model can carry.** R-15-167 makes it plain memory at a fixed range with no fill, no eviction, no valid or tag array, and no lookup that is not the ordinary address decode, so it is declared beside every other property the composition fixes about a range. That leaves one irreducible new fact, *whose* it is, everything else being a refusal over a field the PMA already had: not coherent, not executable, not on a latency class. R-15-165's own test makes the thinness correct rather than lossy, the contents being a function of an explicit software placement and never of access history.
      * **It is on neither latency class, and stating that relaxes an M0.14 invariant rather than adding a third class.** R-15-247's two classes are two media of *main* memory and R-15-168 makes the scratchpad the local tier above them, so the validator's *every array is on exactly one class* becomes *every array that is not a scratchpad*. A third `MemoryClass` arm would misstate R-15-247's own count, move the figures K-60 holds, and give `memory_class_at` a value no latency constant stands behind.
      * **The matrix half of `vmclear`'s inventory is empty, an enumeration result rather than a milestone's residue.** M0.6h booked two absent members; one lands and the other turns out to be nothing to clear, since R-15-117 keeps de-quantization in software, the tile file is not imported, and R-15-118's coprocessor line leaves the unit no state of its own. What a partition can dirty is the scratchpad and nothing else.
      * **The scratchpad has no size, and no configuration in this tree declares one.** R-15-108 selects scratchpad sizes and the exploration has not run, which is what `frozen` is false for in every class row; a range written into a shipped configuration ahead of it is how a placeholder becomes an architectural input. So the model carries the shape, refuses a wrong one, and carries no range.
      * **The scratchpad is unreachable from the C-class golden emulator, so this item adds no corpus member and its harness installs its own address map.** The frozen emulator is the C class (M0.10) and R-15-165 puts the scratchpads on the V and M datapaths, so no corpus program can address one. The three new `$[test]` properties install a region above every region the configurations declare and restore what they found: a property about a structure no shipped configuration has is a property that must supply it.
      * **A new per-region property does not move the key-set gate, and schema conformance catches it instead.** `memory.regions` is a *list*, so a member's properties are not keys of the configuration tree and both declarations agree at 270 before and after; what bites is the schema, which grew the property and refuses a region without it in either file. An unmoved key count is therefore not evidence that nothing was added to a configuration.
      * **The build tree is one tree, and a second worktree's build fails at `configure` rather than at compile.** `cmake` records the source directory in its cache, so a lane building the canonical build directory from a different checkout is refused with a cache mismatch before a line is compiled. The lever needed no tool change, [vos/env.py](../../tools/vos/env.py) already reading `VOS_BUILD_DIR` and `VOS_LOG_DIR`, and it is the mechanism by which two items that both write `model/` serialize.
      * **This item owes the checker no rule, because the fact it would hold is a model fact rather than a document one.** The one cross-artifact agreement it creates is between a class's declared `scratchpad_bytes` and the address map's declared range, both in the configuration the Sail validator reads, so the refusal fires on every run of every loop rather than on a document sweep. A checker rule over the same pair would be a second reader of one file.

### M1 · Toolchain spine (incl. the CHERI-CompCert prerequisite)

Build the *functional* CHERI-RISC-V CompCert backend as the first **target-execution prerequisite** (no purecap CertiCoq GC is needed, the on-device path is GC-free, §0). The host-side **CertiCoq → Wasm** oracle and component authoring proceed independently; the **GC-free on-device lowering** (CompCert-C/VST through CHERI-CompCert; arena extraction via MetaCoq→Rust onto the Rust→CHERI compiler for allocation-light components) joins the backend to produce runnable purecap artifacts from a trivial Gallina program on the M0 emulator.
Ship the frozen-dialect **assembler, linker, and image composer** (§0) in the same milestone; nothing downstream links without them. The bring-up realization of that sentence is M1.4′, the in-tree trio M0.12 already proved on the corpus, with M1.4's LLVM re-homing its hardening replacement behind the gate.
Build the profile-freeze analyzer (M1.8a, landed) and, behind the M8a gate, the backend provenance outputs and the ordered dictionary, outlining, operand-form, bitfield, stack-save, and indexed-address reports against the generated corpus: the freeze decides a profile nothing before the gate is compiled against, R-15-014a having made the provisional one a total compilation target.
Every later milestone is purecap and managed-runtime-free from here.

  * **Dispatch. Start:** M1.2a's measured start-from and M1.9's slot-plan interface. **Owns:** M1.2b owns the backend-local representation; after it lands, M1.2g owns `common/`, M1.2c owns the permission and otype definitions, and M1.2e owns the representability checker, in parallel. M1.2d owns frame and call emission and joins those interfaces; M1.2f owns the differential driver and may prepare its generated inputs and comparator before the backend is ready. Shared extraction lists and build manifests have one integrating owner in the contained repository, not concurrent editors. **Check:** each child's predicate is reviewed before implementation; where only exit-evidence prose exists, make that predicate explicit first. **Join:** M1.2f runs against the integrated b, g, c, d and e outputs and M1.4′, then releases M1.7. No contained compiler source is moved into this checkout.
  * **Split at entry, and the split is by seam rather than by axis**, because the eight axes are a delta against `cheririscV/` and the seams are what the authored backend has to cross in order. The children below **redistribute this cell's own 38 h rather than re-price it**: the six midpoints summed to it exactly at the split, because inventing a larger figure would be guessing where the previous one at least recorded what it was priced against, and **M1.2a has since landed under that figure**, at 3.1 h against its own 4 h, so the subtree summed to 37.1 h until M1.2g entered at 9 h as the seam child the split was short of, which puts it at 46.1 h. **The redistribution is of the midpoint and not of the ends**: the six ranges summed to 23–53 at the split, each being set at its own child's midpoint under the class-X span floor rather than carved out of one range, and with M1.2a landed and M1.2g entered the six still open sum to 26–60, so the M1 subtotal, the grand total and the critical chain all move and `--fix` recomputes them. **The re-price is M1.2a's and it is taken**: that item measures the start-from, declines to move any midpoint or any end on the ground that what it found removes work rather than adding it, and re-states what the figure is a floor over, so the five children below stand on a measured start-from rather than on an unmeasured one.
  * **The start-from is stock CompCert's RISC-V backend, taken at M1.1b**, so what is authored is a purecap backend over that one rather than a re-homing of SECOMP's `cheririscV/`. `upstream/SECOMP` stays pinned and is read for its compartment layer, which is where the deferred robust-preservation theorem is stated. **The delta below is measured against `cheririscV/`, which is what M1.1b weighed and not what this item now takes**, and M1.2a carries the same eight axes re-measured against the start-from, each split into the half that start-from removed and the half it leaves to be authored.
  * **Re-priced at M1.1b, and the two grounds are separable.** The start-from decision removes the *deletion* half of the four replacement axes and leaves their authoring half, which is priced against no measurement of the stock backend at all: that backend has no capability type, no permission field, no otype space and no uninitialized-capability discipline, so the profile's five-bit lattice, four-bit otype space, sentry pair and composition-laid-out frame discipline all still have to be written. **Axis 8 resolves at M1.2a and it resolves as a re-homing**, no stage of the compiler assigning an address to a block and composition-time allocation already being `Genv.alloc_global`, so the axis stops being an omission from the sum: its frame half is priced inside M1.2d and its heap half is two arms of an external-function table in `common/`, which joins the seam that item reported as short a child and which M1.2g now carries. What the decision does buy is the acceptance loop below, generated where the item had none at all, which is the one mitigation this item's authority class has ever had.

Preparation of the component wrapper, input generator and comparator runs beside backend implementation. Staging costs stay here; downstream items consume this backend verdict rather than rebuilding the driver.
  * **The `-dcapasm` work is struck outright.** That printer is 202 `TODO` arms of 258 declared constructors, and completing a pretty-printer for a language about to be replaced is waste. The backend emits a typed instruction stream and [vos/dialect.py](../../tools/vos/dialect.py) encodes it, which is also, free, the S4 provenance sidecar M1.8b needs, the stream being record-shaped already.

The one that took work out and the one that put a comparable amount back are therefore both at zero, which is why the re-take leaves the midpoint standing. The old "add 20–40 h if the artifact is unusable" contingency is retired: its trigger was a build, and the build is green.
  * **The artifact builds on this toolchain, and the ground is a build rather than a populated gitlink.** Staged outside the checkout from the gitlink's own bytes, the pin configures and builds whole: `./configure rv64-linux -ignore-ocaml-version`, then `make depend`, `make proof`, `make extraction`, `make ccomp`, every stage exit 0, 203 `.vo`, and a linked `ccomp`, with `make proof` at 14 min 13 s and 1.9 GB peak over twelve cores shared with three lanes. All 14 files `SECARCH` names compile. Three things the run settles. **The prover is not M1.5's**: `configure` tests `coqc --print-version` against a closed list ending at 9.1.0 and the `rocq-9.1.1` switch carries `rocq` with no `coqc`, so the build takes the distribution's Coq **8.20.1** and needs no `-ignore-coq-version`. OCaml is 5.4.0 against a 4.05-to-4.14 test, which is what `-ignore-ocaml-version` is for. And `make proof` alone fails on a clean tree, the `%.vo: %.v` rule resolving its stem through `VPATH` only once `.depend` exists.
  * **`-dcapasm` emits, and what it emits is what bounds this item.** The flag is wired through `Clflags`, `Driver` and extraction's `print_CapAsm`, and on FP-free C it writes a Gallina program term rather than assembler text. Straight-line register-only code emits clean and nothing past it does: one `int` loop emits a `TODO: __Pbgew__`, a pointer-indexed loop seven such tokens, a struct field access eight, the missing mnemonics being the ordinary word loads and stores through a capability and the comparisons beside them. The pretty-printer is where this sits, and the denominator is the declaration rather than the file: `CapAsm.v` declares **258** instruction constructors (lines 233 to 578), where the 479 `| P…` lines the file carries are those plus every `exec_instr` arm, which is the reading trap. Against 258, **228** have a live arm in `backend/PrintCapAsm.ml`'s 748-line match, **202** of them printing a `TODO: __<mnemonic>__` literal and **26** a term, while the other **30** are double-precision forms with no arm at all, the half of the surface this profile deletes in its own right (R-15-039). That is M1.1's "admittedly incomplete pretty-printer" measured, and the incompleteness is the common case rather than the corner.
  * **The correctness proofs are outside the build and every admit is inside it.** `SECARCH` names 14 of the 33 files; the other 19 are not compiled, six of them the `*proof.v` files. Every `Admitted.` and `admit.` in the directory falls in the 14 that are: **35 `Admitted.` and 10 `admit.`**, in `CapMemory.v` (21 and 7), `OCapValues.v` (10), `CapAsmgen.v` (3) and `CapGlobalenvs.v` (1 and 3). What builds is the capability backend's definitions with its correctness argument stubbed.
  * **The step relation is empty at the pin, which is sharper than the admits and decides what the re-homing starts from.** `CapAsm.v:1845` reads `Definition executeAllowedCap (c : occap) : bool := false.` with a `FIXME` beside it, and that constant gates all five step rules (`:1851`, `:1865`, `:1905`, `:1933`, `:1949`), so **no `step` is derivable and the target language's transition relation is vacuous**. There is no `semantics` record either, the definition that would plug `CapAsm` into CompCert's simulation framework being commented out at `:2052` under a `FIXME priority 1`, which is why the six `*proof.v` files sit outside `SECARCH`. Five capability instructions are declared with their semantics commented out: `PCgt` (`:1369`), `PCgs` (`:1381`), `PCseal_e` (`:1428`), `PCCseal` (`:1424`) and `PCinvoke` (`:1176`), and `CapAsmgen.v:1434` emits `PCgs` as `make_return`'s cross-compartment discriminator, so the generated return path names an instruction the machine does not define. The re-homing therefore starts from a design sketch whose definitions compile rather than from a working translation whose parameters move.
  * **The re-homing delta, and the first thing it settles is the shape of the problem.** `cheririscV/` fixes no 128-bit capability *layout*: the capability is abstract in the Cerise lineage, `OCVmem : permission -> locality -> ptrofs -> ptrofs -> ptrofs -> ocsealable` (`OCapValues.v:262`), carrying base, top and address as three full-width `ptrofs` with no exponent, mantissa or compression anywhere in the directory, so the abstract state is three words wide where the footprint is two. There is accordingly no `cheri_compressed_cap`-equivalent algebra to re-parameterize, and the 128 bits enter only as a *footprint*: a chunk's size and alignment (`CapAST.v:70-71`, `CapMemdata.v:47`, `:109`), a typed slot's count (`CapLocations.v:48-50`), and the sixteen-byte `Q128` (`CapMemdata.v:146`, `:666-669`) whose all-or-nothing fragment check is this model's surrogate for the validity tag. What has to move is the eight axes below rather than field widths.
  * **Axis 1, the capability's storage width, is the one this lane moved, and what it measures is where a re-parameterization's cost actually sits.** Upstream welds CLEN to twice XLEN by reading the capability's width off `Archi.ptr64` (`CapAST.v:57`, `:58`, `:171`), with no `Archi.capwidth` beside it, so a 64-bit machine admits only a 128-bit capability. The lane adds `CapAST.cap128` as a width switch of its own, sets it false, and repoints the **35** sites that select a capability's width at it over six files, `CapAST.v`, `CapMemdata.v`, `CapGlobalenvs.v`, `OCapValues.v`, `CapMachregs.v` and `CapAsmgen.v`, leaving `Archi.ptr64` to mean the pointer width alone. `CTcap` and `CMcap` then resolve to the already-present 64-bit forms, so a capability is 8 bytes at 8-byte alignment, one typed slot and `Q64`'s eight fragments, which is [cap_format.sail](../../model/model/core/cap_format.sail)'s `cap_size` exactly, and `CapAsmgen`'s `opldc`, `opsc` and `opUsc` select `PClwc`, `PCscw` and `PCUscw` where they selected the 128-bit forms. Three of the 35 are unreachable by search and were found by a failing proof: the `OCVcap`-against-`CTany64` arms of `has_type`, `normalize` and `load_result` (`OCapValues.v:550`, `:1724`, `:1790`) name no capability constructor, so no grep for one finds them.
  * **Axis 1 is landed, against a clean tree rather than an incremental one:** `make clean` and the full sequence put all fourteen `SECARCH` files and the 203 `.vo` back at exit 0, `make proof` at 21 min 37 s and 1.9 GB peak, extraction and the link green on top. What it cost is the finding: the definitions move mechanically and the proofs do not. Upstream discharges every capability case by splitting on `Archi.ptr64`, so each split now needs the second boolean beside it, **fourteen lines of tactic repair over two files**, in `normalize_type`, `normalize_idem` and `load_result_rettype` (`OCapValues.v`) and four lemmas in `CapMemdata.v`. Two of those selected residual goals **by index** and the wider split moves them, so both are rewritten to walk the disjunction, `repeat first [ left; reflexivity | right ]`, which survives a further split rather than needing the indices recounted. One `Section Examples` goal drops a `rewrite ARCHI` that has no subterm left and was never load-bearing. **The change reaches the output**: on a C program that spills a capability the emitted term carries `PClwc` twelve times where the pin carried `PClcd`, and the stubbed-arm census moves with it, 52 tokens over 2 constructors becoming 64 over 3, the printer's 26 real arms having been written against the 128-bit selection. On a program that loads no capability the term is byte-identical, which is the honest scope of the demonstration. Two things the axis deliberately does not move: the argument-passing layout (`CapConventions1.v:234`, `:258` advancing an outgoing offset by a hard-coded two slots per argument), which is the ABI rather than the width; and `CapValues.v`, which carries the same three tables outside `SECARCH`, so repointing it would repoint something nothing builds.
  * **Axis 7 is not the cheap deletion it reads as, and the reason is recorded so it is not rediscovered.** `X32` is a **second reserved scratch** rather than a spare: `CapAsm.v:1670` states that no LTL register maps to `X31` or `X32`, and `CapAsmgen.v:919`, `:921` and `:928` use the pair together in the stack-offset sequences (`PCgl_s X32 SPC :: … :: Psubl X31 X32 X31`). Deleting the thirty-third register, which no five-bit field can encode, therefore needs a second scratch found inside 32, which is the ABI question axis 1 already declines to answer rather than a deletion. `DDC` is load-bearing for the same kind of reason: `CapAsmgen.v:1352` and `:1392` load each compartment's heap capability into it across the compartment switch, so removing it is entangled with a heap model this dialect does not have at all (R-08-011). The scalar floating-point file is the only genuinely mechanical part of the axis. What that moves is the order rather than the total: axis 7 is not the cheap one to take first.
  * **Axis 2, the permission field, is a replacement and not a narrowing.** SECOMP's is a twelve-element abstract enumeration `O | RO | RW | RWL | RX | E | RWX | RWLX | URW | URWL | URWX | URWLX` (`OCapValues.v:39-51`) ordered by a hand-written `permFlowsTo` (`:93-140`), crossed with a three-valued locality `Global | Local | Directed` (`:57-60`) under its own `locFlowsTo` (`:65-76`). The profile's is five bits naming one of 32 codepoints, sixteen authority shapes crossed with one global bit, decoded by a total `perms_expand` into a twelve-bit architectural bitmap and narrowed by `perms_narrow`'s largest-admitted-subset search ([cap_common.sail](../../model/model/core/cap_common.sail), lines 88-184). Neither order embeds in the other: the profile carries access-system-registers, the `load-global`/`load-mutable` transitivity pair, `store-capability`/`store-local-capability`, and a `Permit_Seal`/`Permit_Unseal` split no admitted set holds together (R-15-007o), none of which SECOMP has, and SECOMP carries four uninitialized permissions and a `Directed` locality the profile has none of. Locality does not survive as a field at all, `store-local` being bit 5 of the profile's expanded bitmap and riding the permission code (R-15-074), so the `locality` type and its uses across six compiled files go with it. The two together are read on 167 lines of the fourteen compiled files.
  * **Axis 3, uninitialized capabilities, is the axis the calling convention rests on, so deleting it is a design act rather than a deletion.** `URW`/`URWL`/`URWX`/`URWLX` (`OCapValues.v:48-51`) are neither read- nor write-allowed (`:190-200`); a `U` capability's authority is its *cursor* rather than its bound (`CapMemory.v:280`), it reads strictly below the cursor (`:331`), stores at or below it (`:322`), and a store at offset zero advances it (`:356-373`), which is the frontier discipline the frame layout is built on. `CapMemtype.v:123-133`'s `UNINIT` access kind threads it through every load and store, 26 `PCU*` constructors carry it into the assembly (`CapAsm.v:394-421`), and `CapAsmgen.v` compiles **every** stack access through one: `Mgetstack` and `Msetstack` (`:1063-1083`), `transl_load` and `transl_store`'s stack arms (`:1115-1218`), the frame carve `PCUtoPtr_s X31 SPC SPC :: PCsbase SPC SPC X31` (`:1333-1334`), and `store_zeros_routine`'s byte-at-a-time frame zeroing (`:1275-1285`). The canonical stack capability is `URWL` plus `Directed` (`CapGlobalenvs.v:186-190`). The profile has neither: its stack capability is `perms_stack`, the one shape carrying `store-local` ([cap_common.sail](../../model/model/core/cap_common.sail) line 119), over a frame the static memory plan lays out at composition (R-08-011), so the carve, the zero-initialization and the spill discipline are all re-derived rather than edited.
  * **Axis 4, sealing, diverges in the field and in the authority, and axis 5's sentries diverge with it.** SECOMP's object type is `Definition seal := ptrofs` (`OCapValues.v:24`), so the otype space is the whole address space, and sealing authority is a separate seal-capability *object* holding a range and a cursor, `OCVseal : seal -> seal -> seal -> ocsealable` (`:263`), consumed by `seal_capability`/`unseal_capability` against a bounds check on that cursor (`:1480-1496`); there is no `Permit_Seal` and no `Permit_Unseal` anywhere in the directory, and `CapAsmgen.v:1374-1376` gives each *call site* its own otype by offsetting a per-compartment seal key. The profile's otype is four bits naming sixteen classes, three architecturally reserved and thirteen composition-allocated with no runtime type allocation ([cap_format.sail](../../model/model/core/cap_format.sail) line 58, [cap_common.sail](../../model/model/core/cap_common.sail) lines 49-65), and its sealing authority is two permissions the lattice never admits together. SECOMP's sentry is the `E` permission, upgraded to `RX` on installation into PCC while *preserving the cursor* (`OCapValues.v:1472-1476`), so it does not force entry at the base, and global function entries use a second mechanism again, a **sealed** `RX Global` capability (`CapGlobalenvs.v:180-184`); the profile's are two reserved otypes, a forward edge and a backward edge, with `cjalr` admitting each only in its own role (R-15-008, R-15-071).
  * **Axis 6, bounds, is the one place the delta adds an obligation rather than exchanging one.** SECOMP's bounds are exact by construction, three `ptrofs`, so nothing in `CapAsmgen` ever computes a representable alignment and no narrowing can round. The profile's are CHERI Concentrate at 8 and 6 mantissa bits, byte-exact to 128 bytes and rounding outward above that (R-15-007c), and R-15-007k requires **every** narrowing the profile's code emits to be exactly representable, discharged against the slot plan rather than by a runtime instrument, with `CRAM`, `CRRL` and `CSetBoundsExact` unadmitted. That is a side condition on the backend's emission with no upstream analogue. Its input is R-08-011's slot plan, which **M1.9** produces **ahead of this item rather than after it**: [MemoryPlan.v](../../proofs/MemoryPlan.v) carries the plan, the placement rule and the delta, and what M1.9 still owes is its seeded-weakening gate rather than the plan. R-15-007k already owns the obligation and already locates it on the plan, so nothing here is a gap in the register.
  * **Axis 7, the register file, carries one part that has no encoding.** `CapAsm.v:46-53` declares `X1` through `X32`, which with the separately modelled `X0` is **33** integer registers where stock `riscV/Asm.v` declares 32 and where a RISC-V register field is five bits wide, so the thirty-third is unencodable in this dialect or any other. Beside it sit a full 32-entry double-precision file (`:62-70`) the profile deletes with scalar FP along with the 30 constructors naming it (R-15-039), and `DDC` (`:103`) with its six `exec_load_ddc`/`exec_store_ddc` forms (`:1228-1251`), which is the hybrid mode R-15-001c deletes. Two register conventions are broken beside them and both are the pin's rather than the profile's: `SPC`, the stack capability, is `X3` (`:133`) while `CapAsmgen.v:1290` reserves `X3` for the caller's seal key, so `:1318`'s `PCseal X4 SPC X3` seals `X3` with itself, and the declared `SPPC := X2` (`:135`) that RISC-V's ABI would put the stack in is used nowhere. The merged reading is the one thing that already agrees, `CapAsm.v:43-44` modelling exactly the merged integer-and-capability file [reg_type.sail](../../model/model/core/reg_type.sail) carries.
  * **Axis 8, the address model, was named and deliberately left out of the estimate, and M1.2a has since ruled it a re-homing.** SECOMP addresses are `ptrofs` offsets inside CompCert's block model, split three ways by `heap_ptr | stack_ptr | seal_ptr` (`OCapValues.v:401-404`) with `derive_offset` reading the region, over a runtime `Mem.alloc`. The profile is one flat 36-bit physical space (R-15-002a), dense by construction (R-15-002b), with no heap and composition-time allocation. Whether that is a re-homing of the memory model or a replacement of it is what this axis turns on, and the ruling is taken on the start-from's own allocation sites: no stage of the compiler assigns an address to a block, composition-time allocation is already `Genv.alloc_global`, and the run-time allocation the profile removes is a frame block and a builtin pair rather than the `mem` type.
  * **One further finding is recorded because it is load-bearing and is not a difference from the profile but a defect in the pin.** `is_directed_store` (`CapMemory.v:296-304`, mirrored at `CapMemtype.v:320-330`) is the clause that stops a stack capability being stashed below itself, and its own comment says it checks the stored value's maximum read authority against *the address it is stored at*. The pattern variable in `| OCVcap c =>` shadows the function's `c` parameter, so `derive_offset k c ofs` reads the address off the value being stored rather than off the authority it is stored through, and the condition becomes a statement about the value alone. Under the `UNINIT` kind, whose offsets are negative by construction (`CapMemtype.v:126`), it then reads as `a <= a + ofs` and fails for the offsets the discipline uses. Nothing in this repository rests on it; it is recorded so that a lane re-deriving the directed-store rule reaches for the comment rather than the code.
  * **The prerequisite this box did not hold is held, and what each output turns out to be is the finding.** The generated `compcert.ini` puts `-mabi=lp64d` on the preprocessor, assembler and linker and no `riscv64` cross toolchain is installed, so `ccomp` reaches a host `gcc` that rejects the flag; relaxing the **preprocessor** line alone is what `-S` needs, the other two waiting on the cross toolchain M1.4 stands up. On a C program shaped to [`base-integer`](../../corpus/base-integer.s)'s and [`base-memory`](../../corpus/base-memory.s)'s subjects, `ccomp -S -dcapasm` exits 0 and writes both. The `-S` half is 87 lines of stock lp64d RISC-V carrying **no capability mnemonic at all**, `-S` reaching the `riscV/` backend rather than `cheririscV/`, so it is a baseline to diff against rather than purecap output. The `-dcapasm` half is a 280-line Gallina term carrying **56 `TODO: __<mnemonic>__` tokens over 38 distinct constructors**: the long-word arithmetic and branches (`Paddl` ten times, all six `Pb*l` forms), the capability loads and stores (`PClb` through `PCsw`), and the uninitialized stack forms the frame discipline runs on. No corpus member is an input to either, all twenty-five being hand-written dialect assembly where `ccomp` takes C. One limit of the baseline: on a function with twelve pointer arguments the **stock** `backend/PrintAsm.ml` raises an uncaught `Failure "not implemented"` where `-dcapasm` writes its term, so the baseline is narrower than the capability path it is meant to be diffed against.
  * **The differential rig's shapes do not meet, and what is missing is an executor rather than a corpus.** [differential-corpus.md](../assurance/differential-corpus.md) §6 adjudicates two executors record by record, and neither `ccomp` output is a record stream. Three things stood between them when the delta was taken against `cheririscV/` and **the start-from decision removed two of them.** The vacuous step relation and the 33-register file with a `DDC` are the pin's, and an authored backend over stock `riscV/` inherits neither: its semantics are written rather than taken, and its register file is the profile's merged 32 by construction. What stands is the encoder from the emitted stream to the frozen dialect's binary, which is **M1.4′**'s. What this item has beside it is the build, the delta, the axis above, and the `-S` baseline the authored output will diff against.
  * **Two duties R-18-014a places on this backend are scoped in here rather than discovered later.** Backend *completeness* includes selecting the frozen capability indexed load and store (R-15-007e) and bitfield extract and insert (R-15-067a) wherever the pattern matches, and three of those rows are decided at the **final freeze**: the indexed form's scale immediate against the emitted mix (R-15-007g), and the bitfield pair's field-specifier form, whether the insert form is carried, and **whether the pair is carried at all** (R-15-067d). Each is a selection pattern written against a row that can move and one may be deleted outright; R-15-014a settles the circularity by making the provisional profile a total compilation target, so what is left is rework at the margin, priced inside the range above. The second duty, **outlining and tail merging** (R-15-036o, R-18-003c), presupposes a backend already emitting correct purecap code and is **M1.3a** rather than a placement inside this item.
  * **Gate satisfied at M1.1a: contained.** The backend is a build-time producer this repository never redistributes, so the re-homing may proceed. Two things follow into this cell: the work must not create a path by which the backend or a derivative of it is conveyed, and the cell prices the re-homing alone. The forfeit, an image a third party cannot rebuild, is M1.1a's to state and M1.4's and R-05-023a's to live with.
  * **The containment decides the shape of this item's evidence, and leaves one question open that is not technical.** A re-homed `cheririscV/` is a derivative of the backend, so it cannot live in this repository under [COPYRIGHT.md](../../COPYRIGHT.md)'s map: every measurement above is taken in a build lane outside every checkout and reported here rather than landed as a diff. **The re-homed tree lives at `c:\Users\symbi\source\repos\verifiedos-cheri-compcert`, a local git repository with no remote, published nowhere**, on the same footing as the CHERI-QEMU fork M2.1 stands up, so the non-commercial term is untouched and containment holds. Its first commit is the pin extracted verbatim and the axis above is its second, `1e79ed1`. Every figure in this cell is therefore evidence taken in that repository and is not reproducible from this tree, which carries the pin and no file of the re-homed tree. **What it supplies downstream is a configured and built CompCert 3.17 at the pin, SECOMP's fork whose `riscV/` carries the compartment layer and is the start-from M1.1b names, plus axis 1 as a landed measurement over `cheririscV/`**; F-224 closes on that reading, the start-from being the pin's own `riscV/` because it alone composes with the `Mach` layer and the security layer the robust-preservation deferral keeps, and no stock tree being pinned here; the patch series is a patch series on the directory this item no longer re-homes, so M1.3, M1.6 and M1.7 resume from a built toolchain rather than from a resumable re-homing. One question stays open and it is not technical: whether anybody maintains a fork of a non-commercially-licensed compiler over the life of this plan. **The route-around-it arm is not a second one**: M1.1a re-weighed it against what this item measured, on exactly the ground that arm was priced against a *verified* backend, and it holds with the trigger for re-weighing it again named at that cell.
  * **M1.1a's own re-weigh trigger is not reached, and the cell says so rather than leaving it to a reading.** That trigger is M1.2's midpoint and the bootstrap claim, and neither is met: the only hours booked in this subtree are M1.2a's 3.1 h against a 38 h midpoint, and they are a re-baselining of the delta rather than progress through the authoring, as axis 1's landing under the previous start-from was a measurement rather than progress through it; nothing since has moved a third-party rebuild onto the critical path. M1.4 records the direction outright, containment leaving the toolchain undistributable so the reproducible-bootstrap ground is not available to be spent, and M1.6 moves it the other way only as far as *a component lowered this way is third-party-rebuildable, and that does not recover the kernel*. What would fire it is a release criterion or R-05-023a's instrument citing a rebuild by an independent party as evidence rather than as hygiene, which is a licensing and product call and is not taken here.

      * **The pin's recomposition proof does not build against a backend without the float bank**, and that is where the deletion's cost actually lands. `security/Recomposition.v` enumerates the instruction set in sibling bullets and names the inversion products of the two load-argument step rules positionally, so deleting three float store forms and narrowing `Pld_arg`'s destination from `ireg + freg` to `ireg` moves both.

The exception waives the notice conditions of Apache §4(a), (b) and (d) for portions embedded in object form by compilation, which is the clause a compiler's own runtime pieces raise rather than one this item spends. The gitlink is unpopulated on purpose, this item not having started; a clone that needs it runs `git submodule update --init upstream/llvm-project`.

This repository already holds such a specification twice, in the model's `encdec` clauses and in [vos/dialect.py](../../tools/vos/dialect.py)'s mnemonic table, which is what makes it worth weighing.

Both artifacts are CompCert extensions, so the first question at the milestone that would take either is whether the **INRIA Non-Commercial License Agreement** reaches them, read from each artifact's own licence file at that milestone against the containment arm M1.1a took. Neither is CHERI, so a capability-carrying encoding, a tag-bearing image and the sealed forms stay authored. Neither is this dialect, so the RISC-V instantiation is a template to re-home rather than a backend to adopt, the same act M1.2 prices for the compiler. And whether this or R-05-023a's decompilation into logic is the covering instrument for the source-correspondence theorem is a call at the hardening program's opening: this cell records a candidate and decides nothing.

Nothing here is a new instrument: this is the adjudication [that document's §6](../assurance/differential-corpus.md#6-how-a-run-is-adjudicated) already runs, applied to the first image this plan lowers rather than assembles.

Assembling is not one of the two: **K-51** assembles every corpus member on every host run, so a stream [vos/asm.py](../../tools/vos/asm.py) refuses is already a finding at the membership above rather than a second condition here. What discriminates is the trace and the reproduction. The trace carries at least one `W` record whose tag is set and a matching `R` record reading that address back tagged, which is [the corpus document's §4](../assurance/differential-corpus.md#4-the-commit-trace-schema-version-1) saying the program put a capability through memory and got it back rather than moving one only inside the register file; and the composed image reproduces byte-for-byte from the same sources and pins **on a lane other than the one that built it**, which is M1.4′'s own discipline and the one property a run inside its own worktree cannot decide.

### M2 · Fast emulator, struck

The CHERI-QEMU fork (§10) carried two jobs and neither is now its own. **The second executor is R2's**, TestRIG adjudicating one RVFI-DII trace format across the golden model and the RTL under Verilator (S11), which arrives on the same schedule and cross-checks the artifact that ships rather than a second model. **The first, the daily driver, is the Sail emulator itself**, at the rate S4 measures, which is taken before this strike is relied on rather than after.

**So M2.1 through M2.4 are struck and carry no estimate**, and what stood behind them was 64.5 h of class-X work, a fork of a tree five major releases behind its own upstream whose landed reconnaissance records 79 unimplemented symbols across four families of state the frozen format does not carry.

**Both rulings are discharged and the strike stands.** The first was a measurement and S4 took it: the emulator retires 71 to 107 kIPS untraced and 66 to 100 traced at the frozen profile, and *through* M6 nothing this plan runs is emulator-bound at that rate, the RVFI-DII rig being socket-bound between ten and forty times below it and the whole differential corpus being 8,830 records. What is left is the composed boot, which does not exist until M7.1, and S4 states the instruction budget that boot has to fit inside for this reading to hold and names M7.1 as what measures it against the budget. The second is taken at M4.4 as single-instance bring-up, the multikernel's defining property being first exercisable on the RTL track where a second implementation already stands, so no reinstatement rides it either. Reinstating costs the pin and nothing else: both upstreams stay gitlinks, and the containment rule below is unchanged by the strike. **What is not a trigger is the emulator feeling slow**, which is the reading this paragraph exists to refuse: what reopens M2 is a boot over S4's budget, and a strike re-argued without one is a strike re-argued from impatience.

The measured state of M2.1 stands as the record of what was done, because it is evidence rather than a plan: the library is narrowed, the 64+1-bit instantiation agrees with the Sail model over 21,546 model-emitted vectors with no disagreement, and the fork is cut as far as anything compiles before a decode surface exists.

  * **Revocation interface:** consume Q22a's resident-register, saved-context and loan-completion predicate on the single-hart path; an epoch mark alone cannot witness semantic completion. Connect the barrier-sanitized saved image or defined filtered load result to `PartitionContext`'s exact value/tag restoration: total restoration alone establishes no removal of stale authority. Price any additional runtime or oracle work at entry. R2 owns the multi-hart/proxy join, and this item's existing partition/switch tests do not claim that evidence.

Reuse the corpus driver and generated register inventory. Return handoff or compiler defects to their owners instead of repairing shared producer files concurrently.

Start:** M4.2's landed statements, M4.3's executable oracle and a reviewed ABI, primitive and initial-capability interface agreed with M1.2d and M3.5. **Owns:** GC-free kernel C and its partition, switch and executive test member. Author and host-check these while the compiler and firmware lanes run; do not wait for M3.5's full target run to begin writing C. **Check:** local logic against the existing oracle, then all clauses below through `run.py model corpus`. **Join:** M1.2f's backend, M1.7's target path and M3.5's actual initial-capability handoff. Fixture-backed results do not close this item.
  * **The multi-hart ruling is taken and this item is single-instance bring-up.** The Sail emulator runs one hart (R-15-005, M0.7, M0.8a) and all three shipped configurations compose one, so the multikernel's defining property, disjointness of duplicated instances, is first exercisable on the RTL track and is deferred there rather than emulated here. What that buys is honesty about what M8a demonstrates: the composition is not validated at the software gate and the plan says so, where the arm not taken would have bought a second *model* to exercise it against, which is the job R2 now does over one trace format against a second *implementation*.
  * **Re-priced upward because M4.1b's arm forecloses the start-from.** The C is authored against M4.2's Gallina specification rather than started from CHERI-seL4's purecap C, so the class stays X and the midpoint moves with the authoring; the range is widened against the class-X outturn under the width test the conventions now state.
  * **Acceptance predicate, stated on the authority axis because the ruling above removes the duplication axis.** The item closes when the instance boots under the composed initial capability distribution the plan's §3 supplies and one run of `run.py model corpus` over a member authored for it answers three questions.
    * **The root is the partition's.** R-07-006 has composition-time disjointness enforced at runtime by CHERI bounds and monotonicity letting the instance derive nothing outside its partition, and R-07-005's acceptance clause makes the *composition-time* half a build-time artifact rather than a review, so what a run decides is the runtime half. The member attempts a derivation past the root's top and past each declared shared window's, and checks that each is refused, reporting through HTIF as every member does. The check sits in the program rather than in the trace reader on the corpus document's own ground: base and top are the decode of the bounds triple and the rig deliberately does not re-derive them, a second implementation of the bounds algorithm inside a reader being a second place for it to be wrong.
    * **The switch is total.** R-07-015's restore is stated in [PartitionContext.v](../../proofs/PartitionContext.v) over the one merged file of 32 registers of 64+1 bits R-15-007i fixes and over every CSR a partition can name, restored or zeroized per [the profile](../hardware/isa-profile.md)'s own disposition. The run's obligation is that the `X` and `C` records between two slot boundaries are exactly that set with their tags, and carry nothing else: a restore truncated to the low registers, one dropping a validity tag, and one exempting a nameable CSR are three of that file's own refuting constructions, so the measurement and the statement are one enumeration read from two sides.
    * **The frame is the table's, and what a run decides is its order rather than its duration.** R-07-032 makes the executive table-driven with no runtime scheduling decision, and a runtime decision is observable as a departure from the declared order: the obligation is that each reserved slot is entered once per major frame and none twice, that the switches fall in the table's own sequence, and that no switch falls where the table names no boundary, read off the `I` records' `pc` against the partition extents the composition declares, with the restore bursts the question above enumerates marking the boundaries. It is the trace's measurement and not a host clock's, which is the column [the freeze contract's §9](freeze-measurement-contract.md#9-the-freeze-gate) bars a report from carrying. **The duration is not a run's to take on this machine and is already discharged where R-11-006 puts it.** `slot_bound` sits in `slot_width`'s and `switch_cost`'s unit in [CyclicExecutive.v](../../proofs/CyclicExecutive.v), which is cycles, and R-11-015 derives it as a static max-path sum rather than measuring it; [the corpus document's §4](../assurance/differential-corpus.md#4-the-commit-trace-schema-version-1) gives the `I` record order, `pc` and the decoded word and no cycle field; and R-15-077 deletes `mcycle`, `minstret` and both counter banks in every lifecycle state, so [core/timing.sail](../../model/model/core/timing.sail) states outright that no counter exists to read a cycle from. R-11-006's acceptance clause makes the schedulability check a Coq artifact rather than a report and [CyclicExecutive.v](../../proofs/CyclicExecutive.v) is that artifact, with R-11-009's switch duty inside it, so a retire count held against a cycle bound would restate a discharged obligation in a unit this machine does not carry.
  * **What one hart cannot decide, and it is the item's own name.** *Per emulated core* is one instance here, so R-07-003's identical text at strictly disjoint state and R-07-004's absence of shared mutable kernel data have no second instance to be false of and no measurement on this emulator reaches them; they are R2's, over one trace format against a second implementation. A green M4.4 says the instance is bounded by its partition, its switch is total, and its slots run in the table's order, and says nothing about two instances being disjoint.

  * **Acceptance predicate, in two halves, and only the first is writable today.**
  * **The half that is writable is the one two instances make measurable and one cannot.** Three questions, answered by one run of `run.py model corpus` over the composed image. R-10-003 has L1 one parametric index verified once and instantiated per object class, so the obligation is that the image carries **one** index body at two instantiations, which the composer's link map decides outright and which a second body fails; R-10-001 has every read of the base runtime-verified against the boot-attested root, so the system-integrity instance's read of an object whose hash does not match is refused and the refusal reaches the HTIF verdict rather than the returned bytes; and R-10-016 makes the per-domain digest deterministic within a domain, incomparable across domains and uncomputable without the domain key, so a payload written byte-identically into both instances yields two digests that differ. The third is the measurement this item's own name buys and is the reason the item is two instances rather than one: a single instance cannot take it at all, and R-10-016's acceptance clause states the property as impossible by construction rather than forbidden by convention, which is exactly the shape a two-domain run decides.
  * **The half that is not writable, and two things stand in the way rather than one.** First, no entry chooses a recovery discipline: [JournalIndex.v](../../proofs/JournalIndex.v) reports that gap and exhibits both arms, each proved to keep every obligation the other keeps, with the two reaching different stores from one crashed journal, so a predicate naming a post-crash store would take the register's decision instead of measuring anything. Second, and simpler, the emulator answers no access at the window a storage instance would run against: all three shipped compositions declare `platform.blkdev`, the attested devicetree advertises `verifiedos,block-device`, and [sys/platform.sail](../../model/model/sys/platform.sail)'s MMIO dispatch claims no such window, so a read falls through to the RAM path and answers zero rather than refusing. [This plan's §7](#7-filesystem-coq) prices the item against a *modeled block device*; until one exists there is nothing for a crash-and-recovery predicate to be taken over, and writing one now would be the failure M1.8a names, criteria authored against an output that cannot yet contradict them.

**M5 subtotal:** 64 h · 4% · 14 h complete · open range 32–68 h.

### M6 · Userland spine

Init/supervision tree (§8) brings up the reference components; admission checker (§9) validates the package set; the package composer emits the finite typed handler/translator graph and pre-admitted media templates, and the contained object router exercises private namespaces, intents, live queries, deterministic translation caching, and protocol-bound credential handles over the existing IDL and rings.
The ring data plane is brought up in its contract order: the common ring schema and lifecycle authored in the IDL profile with the reference client/server bindings and Coq interface skeleton generated from it, then one copy-based service carrying the SPSC, notification, and capacity proofs, then one DMA service adding the extent, cancellation, teardown, and quiescence proofs, so the contract's constants and generated-proof interfaces are validated on two real services before every other server rides them. No service is grandfathered: one that cannot state its finite capacities, lifecycle semantics, cleanup bounds, and per-operation WCET is not admitted through the ring profile.

    * **Acceptance predicate over three of the router's five subjects, and a stated refusal over the other two.** The three are the ones whose entries carry an acceptance clause that decides; the two are the ones M6.3a already reported the register does not close.
    * **Private namespaces (R-10-005b).** That entry's acceptance clause is three prohibitions and each is a measurement rather than a review: no capability is written into a key, so no `W` record inside a declared index extent carries a tag; no index spans confidentiality domains, which the composed image's declared extents decide; and no query returns an object capability not derivable from the presented namespace capability, which the member checks by presenting one, attempting the widening, and reporting the refusal through HTIF.
    * **Live queries (R-10-005c).** Its acceptance clause fixes a composition-time result and queue bound and makes the subscription volatile, so two runs decide it: one that publishes past the declared queue bound emits exactly one rescan-required marker and no further delta and never backpressures the committing transaction, and one that reboots finds no subscription surviving and re-establishes it by rescan. Both are read off the ring's own indices and the member's HTIF verdict, and neither wants a figure a reader computes.
    * **Protocol-bound credential handles (R-12-015a).** A returned credential capability is sealed and non-exportable and carries every one of the seven bindings that entry enumerates, and a holder attempting to widen the protocol role, the peer or origin scope, or the permitted operation is refused, which is that entry's acceptance clause stated as a program rather than as a reading.
    * **Partial source artifact: [CredentialHandles.v](../../proofs/CredentialHandles.v).** The credential package supplies executable schema, binding, use, expiry and trusted-consent guards, monotone attenuation of finite scopes and operations, and serial charging of a shared server-owned account. Its policy and authentic handle lookup are explicit service inputs; the fixture selects no platform clock, protocol vocabulary or IDL. At source commit `98b0c5cfe55d98fe7f70a33023af0e151a4e52c9`, `run.py seed coq --file proofs/CredentialHandles.v --jobs 1` restricted by `--region` to `bindings_match`, `schema_accepts`, `consent_gate`, `authorization_checks`, `authorized`, `delegation_checks`, `delegates`, `charge`, `authorize_and_charge` and `authority_allows` kills all 17 selected mutants out of the file's 114 generated sites, with no survivors or stillborn mutants. The kills are prover rejections under Rocq 9.1.1, not changes to the existing Gallina-front execution vectors. Authentic sealed handles, the concrete protocol IDL and cryptography, consent and time services, atomic charging, and the storage/ring target joins remain open. This work gives no independent completion credit or whole-entry discharge.
    * **Intents and the deterministic translation cache have no predicate, and the ground is the register's rather than this item's.** M6.3a reported that no entry enumerates the intent variants R-12-013a calls closed and that no entry says whether a graph may carry two edges matching one intent; [HandlerGraph.v](../../proofs/HandlerGraph.v) exhibits a first-match and a last-match selector for the second reason, each proved to keep every obligation the other keeps and the two differing observably on an ambiguous graph. A cache keyed on an intent therefore has no closed vocabulary to quantify over and no settled selection discipline to be deterministic against, so a predicate written now would pick an arm and call it a measurement. Both are register acts, both are open at their own entries, and neither is closed here.

    * **Acceptance predicate at the artifact, and a stated gap at the machine.**
    * **At the artifact the predicate is R-18-037's own acceptance clause read as the measurement.** That clause has the suite generate each interface world's tests from the artifact's own constants and names five families: fill to capacity and one past it, cancellation and reset in every lifecycle state, duplicate and stale identifiers, the notification race at the sleep boundary, and **maximum-segment DMA**. All five are instantiated at M6.4's reference world already, the segment arithmetic included: [the declaration](../../interfaces/ring-reference.json) fixes `max_segments` at 4 and `segment_max_bytes` at 1024 and gives every operation a `max_segment_count`, and [RingContract.v](../../proofs/RingContract.v) carries `the_declared_batch_and_segment_maxima_are_attained` and `the_payload_is_exactly_the_declared_segments` over them. **What no landed declaration carries is a DMA world**: the reference world's five operations are extents the server itself serves, and R-12-100's engine is a second initiator none of them names. So the item closes when that declaration carries this world's DMA variant, `run.py ring check` reports RingContract.v byte-identical to what the emitter writes from the declaration and the register entries it reads, and the campaign compiles with all five families instantiated at this variant's own constants.
    * **What the variant adds at the artifact is R-12-100's three clauses minus what the reference world already carries of them**, which is what makes this a re-instantiation rather than a second campaign. Added: the session-table capability whose permissions match the descriptor's declared direction, a direction the reference world declares per buffer reference and states no permission against, with the complete extent validated before the transfer starts and never reinterpreted after; each segment checked independently, where the reference world's validation cost is declared per operation and not per segment; and no capability retained past terminal completion, which is R-12-099's *old capabilities dead* read at the artifact. Carried already: the bounded segment list at its fixed maximum with the payload charged there, in the two theorems above; R-12-097's declaration whole, its cleanup bound, DMA-quiescence rule and maximum time to terminal completion each declared per operation and an operation without the declaration non-cancellable, in `op_quiescence_bound`, in the `cancellation_interval` whose comment names the DMA quiescence, and in the theorems holding cancellability to the declaration, the commit point among the declared points, and that interval inside the declared time to terminal completion; and the stale half of R-12-099's restart test, in `a_stale_generation_is_refused`. What stays outside the artifact however it is stated is the session fail-stopping when its DMA misses the quiescence bound, which is a run's answer and not a declaration's.
    * **The declared margin is where a weakening has to land, and the reference world already puts it at every quantity this variant re-declares.** Fifteen of M6.4's first twenty-four sampled mutants survived as incremented composition constants an inequality absorbed, and what closed them was a declared slack the artifact states an equality against, so a weakening moves a figure instead of eating headroom. The payload bound, the segment maximum and the quiescence bound each carry that shape today, in the payload stated as exactly the declared segments, in the batch and segment maxima attained by a named operation, and in the cancellation interval spending the declared time to terminal completion, and the DMA variant inherits all three from the same emitter. **Three of M6.4's obligations are still plain inequalities**, completion capacity against the maximum simultaneously accepted, the batch against the capacity, and the commit point among the declared points, and they absorb an incremented constant in this world exactly as they do in the reference one, so what is owed there is owed at the declaration and not at this item.
    * **At the machine there is no predicate, and the reason is an absence rather than a decision.** R-12-100's acceptance clause has the engine present the capability to the fabric for every access (R-12-009, R-15-206) and a non-capability DMA write clear the tags of every granule it covers (R-15-183). This model has one hart and no second bus initiator: *DMA engine* occurs once in [sys/platform.sail](../../model/model/sys/platform.sail), in a comment naming what the reset hold covers, and every access it makes is an instruction's. So no run on this emulator decides either half, and the on-machine measurement arrives with a modelled engine or not at all.

### M7 · Full emulated system

The composed stack boots in the spec's §9 order on the **Sail emulator** (§10). Component proof and oracle checks continue independently. M7.1 owns the composed run and its reproduction; host CI does not replace that guest evidence.
The composed system is also the first artifact able to measure **allocation churn**, so the measurement is booked here: the static-composition low-churn argument and the static-code-overlay deferral both wait on the same measured roster, and the elective applications widen the measurement later rather than gating it.
The same roster carries the ring-parameter measurement: queue depths, batch sizes, notification cadence, and slot budgets are read off the composed system against the §12 per-operation accounting, so the contract's constants are chosen from a measured roster rather than estimated per service.

Prepare the boot harness while components develop, integrate one handoff at a time, and return local defects to producers. Publish one image/recipe/digest record for R3, the roster measurements and Q10.
  * **Dispatch. Start:** review the executable roster, entry points, image recipe and expected event digest while the component lanes run. **Owns:** composition and the end-to-end boot harness, including the executable realization of any roster member that exists only as a Gallina statement. Assign that lowering explicitly within this item at entry, or re-price children; do not treat a landed statement as running code. **Check:** the composed boot and corpus acceptance, with component-local failures returned to their owners. **Join:** M3.5's boot chain, M4.4's kernel, M5.3's storage and the executable supervision, composition-time admission and copy-service path. M7.2 and M7.3 may prepare instrumentation now and measure independently against the same accepted roster revision; M8a waits for both.
  * Re-priced once and then half back: the second executor is struck, so the boot is one machine rather than two adjudicated by hand, and the rerun is a clause of this item rather than an unattended runner's, S14 having declined on a measurement. The cell is deliberately not re-priced for it here, a rerun being a command rather than a build, and whether that reading holds is this item's own to take at entry.

Collect distinct activity only where required; do not repeat the full boot acceptance for an unchanged capture.

### RTL track

The RTL track, in parallel from the M0 freeze:

  * Split at entry, and the split is by what each part is written against rather than by [the delta's](../hardware/rtl-reparameterization-delta.md) three kinds, which sort the imported datapath and reach only two of the three children. R1a is the delta's third kind alone, *authoring against the model*, and it needs no imported file, no datapath and no gitlink, so it proceeds against a definition already in the tree. R1b is the delta's other two kinds together, following a parameter and replacing the constants a parameter does not reach, plus the blocks no configuration removes, and it is where the per-site count lives. R1c is the devices beside the core, which the delta does not read at all and which have no definition in this repository yet. Re-priced at the split, and the two open children come to more than the cell they replace, which is what the delta measured rather than a slip: `CLEN = 2 * XLEN` collapses across the 25 files of `core/` the delta books for it, and the caches that dominate the item have no disabling parameter at all.

What is not yet curated is the datapath itself: the caches, the store buffer, and the platform devices.

  * [ ] **R1c · The RoT core and the required platform devices**
    * **Split at entry, and the split is by owner rather than by size.** The address map is a composition act R-15-002b already owns; the platform devices are route-(c) references from a pinned tree; and the RoT core is neither, being the one block on this milestone with no fitting start-from, so it leaves this item entirely for the route-(a) group. The start-froms the other two want are pinned and untouched: `lowRISC/mocha` supplies a bus, a boot ROM, a UART and a top, and R1a is landed beside them, the capability format's algebra cross-checked at 658,659 vectors with no disagreement and twelve seeded defects killed.
    * **These had no definition in this repository to be authored against**, which is what the split answers: there is no SoC top and no bus for them, and R-15-092's enumeration of the net-new blocks does not name them while its *fixed-function sequencers* clause plausibly reaches part of what a link layer would be. **The address map is the half that is now written**: [the frozen profile's composition](../../model/config/verifiedos.json) declares the serial port, the block device and the boot ROM's extent beside the windows it already carried, each of them carried into the attested devicetree and refused where a composition places it wrong (R1c-i). No count of the apertures is stated here, that being a figure nothing computes and the defect F-205 named. The **SoC top follows the map** rather than the other way round, which is why splitting them is what unblocks this: a top authored before the map fixes the map by implication, which is the defect this plan names one milestone over at M6.4.
    * **The RoT core leaves this item, and what decides it is that §2's uniformity argument has three legs and none of them is the pipeline.** They are that one Sail model and one CHERI-CompCert backend cover it, that the capability format is shared so the boot handoff crosses no format seam, and that 64-bit reach keeps measured boot clear of the 4 GiB windowing a 32-bit root would need. Each is an **architecture**-layer property and each is already carried: one model parameterized by class, one authored format package R1a cross-checks against it, and a composed RoT configuration this repository already runs at hart 15 (M3.1). Microarchitecture is per-class by construction, R-15-108 freezing issue width and pipeline depth per class, so two classes with different pipelines is this design's ordinary case rather than an exception. Realizing the RoT as a minimal configuration of the curated C-class datapath would assert a fourth leg §2 never argues for.
    * **Read the other way the proof argument runs against sharing rather than for it, which is the reverse of how the uniformity claim is usually spent.** R-15-097 states that no RTL ⊑ Sail artifact exists for a full application-class core and calls that the least-built layer of the stack, so sharing the datapath at the RoT inherits an *unbuilt* proof at the one block R-15-194 puts in the TCB and calls verified. A refinement's cost is the store buffer, the forwarding write buffer, the bus shim, the multi-cycle interlocks and the debug module rather than the instruction count, and an in-order cache-free core carries none of them, which makes a minimal core a different order of difficulty and not a smaller instance of the same one. It is also the one block where R-15-091's primary closing vehicle is reachable today: S7 recorded that what Kami and Kôika ship as example cores is RV32, in-order and cache-free, which is the shape of a minimal root of trust and precisely not the shape of the imported datapath, and §11's own existence proof rides that spine.
    * **A register gap is reported and not closed.** R-15-090's acceptance names three cores, the CHERI-CVA6 front end, Ara and Gemmini, and the RoT core is not one; R-15-092's authored list names three blocks, the DMA fabric, the TDM NoC and the fixed-function sequencers, and the RoT core is not one either. So the block's route is unassigned while R-15-194 puts it in the TCB, and the plan has been reading it onto the imported side by default. Which entry names it is a register act, and a register act reruns the review gate.
    * **What the re-homing costs the arithmetic is stated rather than absorbed.** Route (a) is the deferred hardening §11 names and does not build, so it carries no cell anywhere and the RoT core leaves the grand total with it, exactly as the closing refinement over the other authored blocks already sits outside it. The two children below are what M8b actually gates on, the co-simulation gate being the C-class core and the devices booting the composed image, and §11 saying nothing is owed on route (a) before it.

Specify that composition and its predicate before the corresponding run; a scalar ISA corpus alone does not establish multikernel disjointness.

Start:** the image and trace contracts for boot-harness preparation. **Owns:** the RTL image-boot comparison and artifact publication. **Check:** the console and event digest criterion below. **Join:** R2's accepted RTL and M7.1's actual composed image and reference digest. ### M8–M10 and later

**The software and co-simulation gates close independently.** M8a consumes M7 and its roster measurements. M8b consumes R3, including its comparison against M7.1's image. Neither gate repeats the other's review; changed shared inputs invalidate the affected evidence.

  * The whole base system running in emulation: the composed stack boots in the spec's §9 order on the golden emulator, cross-checked, corpus green, with the roster's allocation-churn and ring parameters measured off it.
* [ ] **M8b · Co-simulation MVP: R3 complete** · 2 h, range 1–3 · 0.1% · I
  * **Execute:** Accept R3's exact artifact and image comparison without waiting for the M8a ceremony. Reuse the corpus and boot evidence at the same identities; changed RTL, configuration or software renews affected checks. Physical macro qualification remains separate.
  * The RTL artifact of record in hand, corpus-green under Verilator and booting the same image to the same console and event digests.
  * Everything after the two is hardware realization and hardening.

  * Synthesize the R3 artifact for the board (§11); the purecap golden-model images (M1–M7) boot on it directly, differentially tested against M7; and the exit criterion is **M0.10's replacement pair rather than an ISA-test suite**, the differential corpus green on the board and the composed image reaching the same console and event digests as the golden emulator. M0.6f excluded `riscv-tests` outright, so *ISA tests green* names a corpus this machine refuses in full, and no item below generates a replacement: §0's Isla derivation of ISA test vectors from the frozen model is the route and it carries no cell.

Integrate and measure one class at a time on the accepted scalar platform.

Where it reaches this item is the amortized check, which M0.8b's per-element discard and R-15-085a's two vector-memory rows both price as though the question were closed, and which is the boundary a magnitude would come from if R-17-041's ever acquire one.

Coordinate shared lab resources rather than serializing preparation behind software gate dates.
  * Close the simultaneous-activation set against measured IR-drop, thermal-coupling, and power-signature limits at composition capacity and at the frozen bank granularity, then feed the admitted set back into the §11 mode-transition budget and R-15-247g.
